# Luma AMD Speedrun: Optimize Kernels — Research-Informed Plan

## Context

Optimize 3 GPU kernels for AMD MI355X leaderboard rankings. Currently scoring 0 points (need top 10 in at least one). All testing is remote-only via Popcorn CLI (~2 min/submit).

| Kernel | Rank | Our Time | Leader | Gap | Leaderboard |
|--------|------|----------|--------|-----|-------------|
| MoE | 34/43 | 185 us | 145 us | 1.28x | `amd-moe-mxfp4` |
| GEMM | 67/68 | 24 us | 9.7 us | 2.49x | `amd-mxfp4-mm` |
| MLA | 40/54 | 191 us | 4.3 us | 44x | `amd-mixed-mla` |

**Priority:** GEMM fix (easiest win) → MoE tuning (closest to top 10) → MLA tuning

## Deep Research Findings (from aiter source code analysis)

### GEMM: `get_torch_quant` bypasses JIT bug
- `fused_moe.py` imports `get_hip_quant` (not Triton) internally
- `aiter/ops/quant.py` has 3 quant backends: `get_torch_quant` (pure PyTorch), `get_triton_quant` (Triton JIT), `get_hip_quant` (HIP C++)
- Our bug: `get_triton_quant` produces different JIT compilations from different call-sites
- **Fix:** Use `get_torch_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)` which maps to `per_1x32_f4_quant` (deterministic PyTorch) — no JIT dependency
- B is already pre-quantized+shuffled in the input data, so we only need to quantize A

### MoE: 5 tunable parameters discovered
From `aiter/fused_moe.py` (1828 lines):
1. **`block_size_M`** — tile size along token dim. Default `-1` auto-selects from CSV configs. Override with `32`, `64`, `128`
2. **`moe_sorting_dispatch_policy`** — default `0`. Controls token-to-expert dispatch strategy
3. **`doweight_stage1`** — applies topk_weights in stage 1 vs stage 2
4. **1-stage vs 2-stage** — auto-selected by `get_2stage_cfgs()`. For MXFP4 `q_dtype_a=fp4x2`: 1-stage when `token > 32 && inter_dim % 256 == 0`
5. **OPUS sorting** — env var `AITER_USE_OPUS_MOE_SORTING=1` switches to `moe_sorting_opus_fwd`
6. **`fused_moe_dp_share_expert`** — AMD's own shared expert module in `aiter/fused_moe_dp_shared_expert.py`. Runs shared expert as separate dense op

### MLA: auto-tuner uses much lower NUM_KV_SPLITS
From `aiter/mla.py` `get_meta_param()`:
- Auto-tuner picks `num_kv_splits` from 1-16 (not 32!) based on: `score = gpu_util * compute_efficiency`
- For FP8, splits capped by `min_block_n` lookup: `{16:128, 32:128, 64:64, 128:32, 256:32, 512:32}` keyed by `nhead * max_seqlen_q`
- `fast_mode=True` in metadata builder allocates `(bs + cu_num) * tiles` work items vs `tile_cnt * cu_num` — much smaller buffers
- Non-persistent mode auto-selects `num_kv_splits` and uses `MAYBE_FINAL_OUT=True` for direct output (skipping reduction when `num_kv_splits==1`)

## Implementation Tasks

### Task 1: Fix GEMM — `get_torch_quant` bypass (target: < 20 us)

**Objective:** Replace `ref_kernel` delegation with direct `gemm_a4w4` call using `get_torch_quant` for A quantization.

**Files:** `kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py`

**Implementation:**
```python
import torch
import aiter
from aiter import QuantType, dtypes
from aiter.ops.shuffle import shuffle_weight
from task import input_t, output_t

def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data
    A = A.contiguous()
    # Use get_torch_quant (pure PyTorch, no Triton JIT) to avoid call-site bug
    quant_func = aiter.get_torch_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
    A_q, A_scale = quant_func(A, shuffle=True)
    out = aiter.gemm_a4w4(A_q, B_shuffle, A_scale, B_scale_sh, dtype=dtypes.bf16, bpreshuffle=True)
    return out
```

**Verify:** `popcorn submit --mode test` passes → `--mode benchmark` < 20 us → `--mode leaderboard`

**Fallback if `get_torch_quant` shuffle kwarg not supported:** Try `quant_func(A)` without shuffle, then `shuffle_weight(A_q, layout=(16,16))` + `fp4_utils.e8m0_shuffle(A_scale)` manually.

**Fallback 2:** Try `aiter.get_hip_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)` — the HIP backend used internally by `fused_moe`.

### Task 2: Optimize GEMM — shape-dependent routing (target: top 40)

**Depends on:** Task 1

**Files:** `kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py`

**Try in order:**
1. **Triton `gemm_afp4wfp4`** for M >= 64 — `aiter.ops.triton.gemm.basic.gemm_afp4wfp4` has split-K support and auto-tuning configs. May outperform CK `gemm_a4w4` for large M
2. **Shape routing:** M <= 32 → `gemm_a4w4`, M >= 64 → `gemm_afp4wfp4`
3. **`splitk` parameter** for `gemm_a4w4` — default heuristic computes from CU count and tile sizes

**Verify:** `--mode benchmark` per config, compare vs Task 1 baseline

**Files:** `kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission.py`

## Risks

| Risk | Mitigation |
|------|------------|
| `get_torch_quant` may not accept `shuffle=True` kwarg | Fall back to manual shuffle: `shuffle_weight()` + `e8m0_shuffle()` |
| MoE gains marginal (already near reference) | Even 5% improvement helps ranking. Try all 5 parameter variations |
| MLA top entries use custom ASM | Target mid-pack (top 25). `fast_mode` + split tuning is realistic |
| Slow iteration (~2 min/submit) | Start with GEMM fix (highest confidence), then parallelize MoE/MLA sweeps |

```
     7→The shared expert processes ALL tokens as a dense operation, while routed
     8→experts go through the sparse MoE dispatch. This can improve GPU occupancy
     9→by converting part of the sparse workload into a dense one.

    11→Fallback: If shared expert specialization is slower (e.g., for configs where
```

```
     1→"""
     2→MLA decode kernel optimized for AMD MI355X.
     3→
     4→Strategy: Use aiter's mla_decode_fwd persistent-mode kernel with fp8 Q + fp8 KV.
     5→This matches the reference approach (2-3x faster than naive torch attention).
     6→Future improvement: MXFP4 KV cache for 2x additional bandwidth savings.
     7→"""
     8→import torch
     9→from task import input_t, output_t
    10→from aiter import dtypes as aiter_dtypes
    11→from aiter.mla import mla_decode_fwd
    12→from aiter import get_mla_metadata_info_v1, get_mla_metadata_v1
    13→
    14→FP8_DTYPE = aiter_dtypes.fp8
    15→
    16→# MLA constants (DeepSeek R1 forward_absorb)
    17→NUM_KV_HEADS = 1
    18→KV_LORA_RANK = 512
    19→QK_HEAD_DIM = 576
    20→V_HEAD_DIM = KV_LORA_RANK       # 512
    21→SM_SCALE = 1.0 / (QK_HEAD_DIM ** 0.5)
    22→PAGE_SIZE = 1
    23→NUM_KV_SPLITS = 32
    24→
    25→
    26→def quantize_fp8(tensor: torch.Tensor) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
    27→    """Dynamic per-tensor FP8 quantization."""
    28→    finfo = torch.finfo(FP8_DTYPE)
    29→    amax = tensor.abs().amax().clamp(min=1e-12)
    30→    scale = amax / finfo.max
    31→    fp8_tensor = (tensor / scale).clamp(min=finfo.min, max=finfo.max).to(FP8_DTYPE)
    32→    return fp8_tensor, scale.to(torch.float32).reshape(1)
    33→
    34→
    35→def _build_metadata(
    36→    batch_size: int,
    37→    max_q_len: int,
    38→    nhead: int,
    39→    nhead_kv: int,
    40→    q_dtype: torch.dtype,
    41→    kv_dtype: torch.dtype,
    42→    qo_indptr: torch.Tensor,
    43→    kv_indptr: torch.Tensor,
    44→    kv_last_page_len: torch.Tensor,
    45→):
    46→    """Build persistent-mode metadata for mla_decode_fwd."""
    47→    info = get_mla_metadata_info_v1(
    48→        batch_size, max_q_len, nhead, q_dtype, kv_dtype,
    49→        is_sparse=False, fast_mode=False,
    50→        num_kv_splits=NUM_KV_SPLITS, intra_batch_mode=True,
    51→    )
    52→    work = [torch.empty(s, dtype=t, device="cuda") for s, t in info]
    53→    (work_metadata, work_indptr, work_info_set,
    54→     reduce_indptr, reduce_final_map, reduce_partial_map) = work
    55→
    56→    get_mla_metadata_v1(
    57→        qo_indptr, kv_indptr, kv_last_page_len,
    58→        nhead // nhead_kv,
    59→        nhead_kv,
    60→        True,  # is_causal
    61→        work_metadata, work_info_set, work_indptr,
    62→        reduce_indptr, reduce_final_map, reduce_partial_map,
    63→        page_size=PAGE_SIZE,
    64→        kv_granularity=max(PAGE_SIZE, 16),
    65→        max_seqlen_qo=max_q_len,
    66→        uni_seqlen_qo=max_q_len,
    67→        fast_mode=False,
    68→        max_split_per_batch=NUM_KV_SPLITS,
    69→        intra_batch_mode=True,
    70→        dtype_q=q_dtype,
    71→        dtype_kv=kv_dtype,
    72→    )
    73→
    74→    return {
    75→        "work_meta_data": work_metadata,
    76→        "work_indptr": work_indptr,
    77→        "work_info_set": work_info_set,
    78→        "reduce_indptr": reduce_indptr,
    79→        "reduce_final_map": reduce_final_map,
    80→        "reduce_partial_map": reduce_partial_map,
    81→    }
    82→
    83→
    84→def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    85→    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config = data
    86→
    87→    batch_size = config["batch_size"]
    88→    nq = config["num_heads"]
    89→    nkv = config["num_kv_heads"]
    90→    dq = config["qk_head_dim"]
    91→    dv = config["v_head_dim"]
    92→    q_seq_len = config["q_seq_len"]
    93→
    94→    # Quantize Q to fp8
    95→    q_fp8, q_scale = quantize_fp8(q)
    96→
    97→    # Use fp8 KV cache (pre-quantized in generate_input)
    98→    kv_buffer_fp8, kv_scale_fp8 = kv_data["fp8"]
    99→
   100→    # Build KV indices (contiguous pages)
   101→    total_kv_len = int(kv_indptr[-1].item())
   102→    kv_indices = torch.arange(total_kv_len, dtype=torch.int32, device="cuda")
   103→
   104→    # Reshape kv_buffer to 4D: (total_kv, page_size, nhead_kv, dim)
   105→    kv_buffer_4d = kv_buffer_fp8.view(
   106→        kv_buffer_fp8.shape[0], PAGE_SIZE, nkv, kv_buffer_fp8.shape[-1]
   107→    )
   108→
   109→    max_q_len = q_seq_len
   110→    kv_last_page_len = (kv_indptr[1:] - kv_indptr[:-1]).to(torch.int32)
   111→
   112→    # Build persistent-mode metadata
   113→    meta = _build_metadata(
   114→        batch_size, max_q_len, nq, nkv,
   115→        q_fp8.dtype, kv_buffer_fp8.dtype,
   116→        qo_indptr, kv_indptr, kv_last_page_len,
   117→    )
   118→
   119→    # Allocate output
   120→    o = torch.empty((q.shape[0], nq, dv), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda")
   121→
   122→    # Run aiter persistent-mode MLA decode
   123→    mla_decode_fwd(
   124→        q_fp8.view(-1, nq, dq),
   125→        kv_buffer_4d,
   126→        o,
   127→        qo_indptr,
   128→        kv_indptr,
   129→        kv_indices,
   130→        kv_last_page_len,
   131→        max_q_len,
   132→        page_size=PAGE_SIZE,
   133→        nhead_kv=nkv,
   134→        sm_scale=SM_SCALE,
   135→        logit_cap=0.0,
   136→        num_kv_splits=NUM_KV_SPLITS,
   137→        q_scale=q_scale,
   138→        kv_scale=kv_scale_fp8,
   139→        intra_batch_mode=True,
   140→        **meta,
   141→    )
   142→
   143→    return o
   144→
```

```
     1→"""
     2→FP4 quant + FP4 GEMM reference: bf16 A, MXFP4 B -> MXFP4 per-1x32 quant A -> gemm_a4w4 -> bf16 C.
     3→Quant logic follows aiter op_tests/test_gemm_a4w4.py (get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)).
     4→"""
     5→import torch
     6→from task import input_t, output_t
     7→from utils import make_match_reference
     8→from aiter import QuantType,dtypes
     9→import aiter
    10→from aiter.ops.shuffle import shuffle_weight
    11→# K must be divisible by 64 (scale group 32 and fp4 pack 2)
    12→SCALE_GROUP_SIZE = 32
    13→
    14→def generate_input(m: int, n: int, k: int, seed: int):# -> input_t:
    15→    """
    16→    Generate random bf16 inputs A [m, k], B [n, k] and quantized MXFP4 B, shuffled B and B_scale.
    17→
    18→    Returns:
    19→        Tuple of (A, B), both bf16 on cuda.
    20→    """
    21→    assert k % 64 == 0, "k must be divisible by 64 (scale group 32 and fp4 pack 2)"
    22→    gen = torch.Generator(device="cuda")
    23→    gen.manual_seed(seed)
    24→    A = torch.randn((m, k), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda", generator=gen)
    25→    B = torch.randn((n, k), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda", generator=gen)
    26→    
    27→    # quantized mxfp4 B
    28→    quant_func = aiter.get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
    29→    B_q, B_scale_sh = quant_func(B, shuffle=True)
    30→    
    31→    # shuffle B(weight) to (16,16) tile coalesced
    32→    B_shuffle = shuffle_weight(B_q, layout=(16, 16))
    33→    return (A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh)
    34→
    35→def run_torch_fp4_mm(
    36→    x: torch.Tensor,
    37→    w: torch.Tensor,
    38→    x_scales: torch.Tensor,
    39→    w_scales: torch.Tensor,
    40→    dtype: torch.dtype = torch.bfloat16,
    41→) -> torch.Tensor:
    42→    """
    43→    PyTorch reference: dequant MXFP4 + E8M0 scale -> f32 -> mm -> dtype.
    44→    Same logic as aiter op_tests/test_gemm_a4w4.run_torch.
    45→    x: [m, k//2] fp4 packed, w: [n, k//2] fp4 packed
    46→    x_scales: [m, k//32] E8M0, w_scales: [n, k//32] E8M0
    47→    Returns: [m, n] in dtype
    48→    """
    49→    from aiter.utility import fp4_utils
    50→
    51→    m, _ = x.shape
    52→    n, _ = w.shape
    53→    # fp4 packed -> f32
    54→    x_f32 = fp4_utils.mxfp4_to_f32(x)
    55→    w_f32 = fp4_utils.mxfp4_to_f32(w)
    56→    # E8M0 scale: [*, k//32] -> repeat 32 along k -> f32
    57→    x_scales = x_scales[:m].repeat_interleave(SCALE_GROUP_SIZE, dim=1)
    58→    x_scales_f32 = fp4_utils.e8m0_to_f32(x_scales)
    59→    x_f32 = x_f32 * x_scales_f32
    60→    w_scales = w_scales[:n].repeat_interleave(SCALE_GROUP_SIZE, dim=1)
    61→    w_scales_f32 = fp4_utils.e8m0_to_f32(w_scales)
    62→    w_f32 = w_f32 * w_scales_f32
    63→    return torch.mm(x_f32, w_f32.T).to(dtype)[:m, :n]
    64→
    65→
    66→def ref_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    67→    """
    68→    Reference: MXFP4 per-1x32 quant on A and B; both PyTorch ref and gemm_a4w4 are given.
    69→    Returns gemm_a4w4 for check_implementation.
    70→    """
    71→    A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data
    72→    A = A.contiguous()
    73→    B = B.contiguous()
    74→    m, k = A.shape
    75→    n, _ = B.shape
    76→    
    77→    # 1) PyTorch impl just for your reference: dequant fp4 + e8m0 -> f32 -> mm -> bf16
    78→    # Per-1x32 MXFP4 quant
    79→    # quant_func = aiter.get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
    80→    # quant_func(x, shuffle=False) -> (dtypes.fp4x2, scale); scale layout matches gemm_a4w4
    81→    # A_q, A_scale = quant_func(A, shuffle=False)
    82→    # B_q, B_scale = quant_func(B, shuffle=False)
    83→
    84→    # gemm_a4w4 expects A [M,K/2], B [N,K/2] as dtypes.fp4x2; A_scale/B_scale [*,K/32] E8M0
    85→    # quant_func returns scale as dtypes.fp8_e8m0; gemm_a4w4 accepts E8M0, no view to uint8 needed
    86→    # slice to exact shapes [m,k_scale] / [n,k_scale] (quant may return padded scale)
    87→    
    88→    # k_scale = k // SCALE_GROUP_SIZE
    89→    # A_scale = A_scale[:m, :k_scale].contiguous()
    90→    # B_scale = B_scale[:n, :k_scale].contiguous()
    91→    # out_torch = run_torch_fp4_mm(A_q, B_q, A_scale, B_scale, torch.bfloat16)
    92→
    93→    # 2) aiter.gemm_a4w4 path: needs shuffled B_q and shuffled scales (see test_gemm_a4w4.py:102-105)
    94→    # Per-1x32 MXFP4 quant
    95→    quant_func = aiter.get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
    96→    A_q, A_scale_sh = quant_func(A, shuffle=True)
    97→    # to be noted, aiter also has other a4w4 implements using triton, https://github.com/ROCm/aiter/blob/main/aiter/ops/triton/gemm/basic/gemm_afp4wfp4.py
    98→    out_gemm = aiter.gemm_a4w4(
    99→        A_q,
   100→        B_shuffle,
   101→        A_scale_sh,
   102→        B_scale_sh,
   103→        dtype=dtypes.bf16,
   104→        bpreshuffle=True,
   105→    )
   106→    return out_gemm
   107→
   108→check_implementation = make_match_reference(ref_kernel, rtol=1e-02, atol=1e-02)
```

```
     1→from utils import make_match_reference
     2→from task import input_t, output_t
     3→import torch
     4→import torch.nn.functional as F
     5→from typing import Dict, Tuple, Optional
     6→import math
     7→
     8→import aiter
     9→from aiter import ActivationType, QuantType, dtypes
    10→from aiter.fused_moe import fused_moe
    11→from aiter.utility import fp4_utils
    12→from aiter.ops.shuffle import shuffle_weight
    13→
    14→
    15→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    16→# Constants
    17→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    18→MXFP4_BLOCK_SIZE = 32
    19→PAD_ALIGN = 256
    20→
    21→
    22→def _pad_to(x: int, align: int) -> int:
    23→    return (x + align - 1) // align * align
    24→
    25→
    26→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    27→# generate_input: produce all tensors needed by ref_kernel
    28→#
    29→# Models DeepSeek-R1 MoE layer shapes:
    30→#   - d_hidden = 7168
    31→#   - d_expert = moe_intermediate_size (full=2048, or TP-split)
    32→#   - E_total = n_routed_experts + n_shared_experts (257 or 33)
    33→#   - top_k_total = nexpertspertoken + nsharedexperts (8+1=9)
    34→#
    35→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    36→def generate_input(
    37→    dhidden: int,
    38→    dexpert: int,
    39→    nroutedexperts: int,
    40→    nexpertspertoken: int,
    41→    nsharedexperts: int,
    42→    bs: int,
    43→    seed: int,
    44→) -> input_t:
    45→    d_hidden = dhidden
    46→    d_expert = dexpert
    47→    n_routed_experts = nroutedexperts
    48→    n_shared_experts = nsharedexperts
    49→    routed_top_k = nexpertspertoken
    50→    total_top_k = routed_top_k + n_shared_experts   # e.g. 8 + 1 = 9
    51→    E_total = n_routed_experts + n_shared_experts    # e.g. 256 + 1 = 257
    52→    M = bs  # number of tokens
    53→
    54→    # Padded dimensions (AITER MXFP4 requires 256-alignment)
    55→    d_hidden_pad = _pad_to(d_hidden, PAD_ALIGN)
    56→    d_expert_pad = _pad_to(d_expert, PAD_ALIGN)
    57→
    58→    config = {
    59→        "d_hidden": d_hidden,
    60→        "d_expert": d_expert,
    61→        "d_hidden_pad": d_hidden_pad,
    62→        "d_expert_pad": d_expert_pad,
    63→        "n_routed_experts": n_routed_experts,
    64→        "n_shared_experts": n_shared_experts,
    65→        "n_experts_per_token": routed_top_k,
    66→        "total_top_k": total_top_k,
    67→        "bs": M,
    68→    }
    69→
    70→    gen = torch.Generator(device='cuda')
    71→    gen.manual_seed(seed)
    72→
    73→    # ── hidden_states [M, d_hidden] ──
    74→    hidden_states = torch.randn(
    75→        (M, d_hidden), device='cuda', dtype=torch.bfloat16, generator=gen,
    76→    )
    77→
    78→    # ── Router: softmax top-k (routed experts only) ──
    79→    router_weight = torch.randn(
    80→        (n_routed_experts, d_hidden), device='cuda', dtype=torch.bfloat16, generator=gen,
    81→    ) / math.sqrt(d_hidden)
    82→    router_logits = F.linear(hidden_states, router_weight)  # [M, n_routed_experts]
    83→    scores = router_logits.softmax(dim=-1)
    84→    routed_weights, routed_ids = torch.topk(
    85→        scores, k=routed_top_k, dim=-1, sorted=False
    86→    )
    87→    routed_weights = routed_weights.to(torch.float32)
    88→    routed_ids = routed_ids.to(torch.int32)
    89→
    90→    # ── Append shared expert(s): always selected, weight = 1.0 ──
    91→    # Shared experts are indexed as n_routed_experts, n_routed_experts+1, ...
    92→    shared_ids = torch.arange(
    93→        n_routed_experts, E_total, device='cuda', dtype=torch.int32
    94→    ).unsqueeze(0).expand(M, -1)                               # [M, n_shared_experts]
    95→    shared_weights = torch.ones(
    96→        (M, n_shared_experts), device='cuda', dtype=torch.float32
    97→    )
    98→
    99→    topk_ids = torch.cat([routed_ids, shared_ids], dim=-1)        # [M, total_top_k]
   100→    topk_weights = torch.cat([routed_weights, shared_weights], dim=-1)  # [M, total_top_k]
   101→
   102→    gate_up_bf16 = torch.randn(
   103→        (E_total, 2 * d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad), device='cuda', dtype=torch.bfloat16, generator=gen,
   104→    ) / math.sqrt(d_hidden)
   105→    down_bf16 = torch.randn(
   106→        (E_total, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad), device='cuda', dtype=torch.bfloat16, generator=gen,
   107→    ) / math.sqrt(d_expert)
   108→
   109→    torch_quant = aiter.get_torch_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
   110→    gate_up_weight, gate_up_weight_scale = torch_quant(gate_up_bf16, quant_dtype=dtypes.fp4x2)
   111→    down_weight, down_weight_scale = torch_quant(down_bf16, quant_dtype=dtypes.fp4x2)
   112→    gate_up_weight = gate_up_weight.view(E_total, 2 * d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad // 2)
   113→    down_weight = down_weight.view(E_total, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad // 2)
   114→
   115→    gate_up_weight_shuffled = shuffle_weight(gate_up_weight, layout=(16, 16))
   116→    down_weight_shuffled = shuffle_weight(down_weight, layout=(16, 16))
   117→    gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled = fp4_utils.e8m0_shuffle(gate_up_weight_scale)
   118→    down_weight_scale_shuffled = fp4_utils.e8m0_shuffle(down_weight_scale)
   119→
   120→    return (
   121→        hidden_states,                  # [M, d_hidden]                              bf16
   122→        gate_up_weight,                 # [E_total, 2*d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad//2] fp4x2  (raw)
   123→        down_weight,                    # [E_total, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad//2]   fp4x2  (raw)
   124→        gate_up_weight_scale,           # [E_total, 2*d_expert_pad, scale_K]         e8m0   (raw)
   125→        down_weight_scale,              # [E_total, d_hidden_pad, scale_K]           e8m0   (raw)
   126→        gate_up_weight_shuffled,        # [E_total, 2*d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad//2] fp4x2  (pre-shuffled)
   127→        down_weight_shuffled,           # [E_total, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad//2]   fp4x2  (pre-shuffled)
   128→        gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,  # [padded, flat]                             e8m0   (pre-shuffled)
   129→        down_weight_scale_shuffled,     # [padded, flat]                             e8m0   (pre-shuffled)
   130→        topk_weights,                   # [M, total_top_k]                           float32
   131→        topk_ids,                       # [M, total_top_k]                           int32
   132→        config,
   133→    )
   134→
   135→
   136→
   137→
   138→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   139→# ref_kernel_pytorch: pure PyTorch implementation (dequant + matmul)
   140→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   141→def _dequant_mxfp4(weight_fp4, scale_e8m0):
   142→    """
   143→    Dequantize MXFP4 weight to float32.
   144→
   145→    weight_fp4:  [N, K//2]  fp4x2  (raw, not shuffled)
   146→    scale_e8m0:  [padded_N, ceil(K/32)] e8m0   (M-dim padded to 256-align by dynamic_mxfp4_quant)
   147→
   148→    Returns: [N, K] float32
   149→    """
   150→    # fp4x2 -> float32 lookup: [N, K]
   151→    w_f32 = fp4_utils.mxfp4_to_f32(weight_fp4)            # [N, K]
   152→    # e8m0 -> float32 power-of-2 scale: [padded_N, scale_K]
   153→    s_f32 = fp4_utils.e8m0_to_f32(scale_e8m0)             # [padded_N, scale_K]
   154→    N, K = w_f32.shape
   155→    # Trim scale rows to match weight rows (scale M-dim is padded to 256)
   156→    s_f32 = s_f32[:N, :]
   157→    # Broadcast scale across block_size=32 columns
   158→    s_f32 = s_f32.repeat_interleave(MXFP4_BLOCK_SIZE, dim=-1)[:, :K]  # [N, K]
   159→    return w_f32 * s_f32
   160→
   161→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   162→# ref_kernel_pytorch: pure PyTorch implementation (dequant + matmul)
   163→# will not run. only for reference
   164→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   165→def ref_kernel_pytorch(data: input_t) -> output_t:
   166→    """
   167→    Pure PyTorch reference: dequantize MXFP4 weights -> bf16 matmul -> SwiGLU -> matmul.
   168→    Uses the raw (un-shuffled) weights.
   169→    """
   170→    (
   171→        hidden_states,             # [M, d_hidden]          bf16
   172→        gate_up_weight,            # [E, 2*d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad//2]  fp4x2
   173→        down_weight,               # [E, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad//2]    fp4x2
   174→        gate_up_weight_scale,      # [E, 2*d_expert_pad, scale_K]          e8m0
   175→        down_weight_scale,         # [E, d_hidden_pad, scale_K]            e8m0
   176→        gate_up_weight_shuffled,
   177→        down_weight_shuffled,
   178→        gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,
   179→        down_weight_scale_shuffled,
   180→        topk_weights,              # [M, top_k]  float32
   181→        topk_ids,                  # [M, top_k]  int32
   182→        config,
   183→    ) = data
   184→
   185→    d_hidden = config["d_hidden"]
   186→    d_expert = config["d_expert"]
   187→    d_hidden_pad = config["d_hidden_pad"]
   188→    d_expert_pad = config["d_expert_pad"]
   189→    M = hidden_states.shape[0]
   190→    top_k = topk_ids.shape[1]
   191→    E = gate_up_weight.shape[0]
   192→
   193→    # Dequantize all expert weights to float32
   194→    # gate_up: [E, 2*d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad] -> trim to [E, 2*d_expert, d_hidden]
   195→    # down:    [E, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad]    -> trim to [E, d_hidden, d_expert]
   196→    gate_up_dq = torch.stack([
   197→        _dequant_mxfp4(gate_up_weight[e], gate_up_weight_scale[e])
   198→        for e in range(E)
   199→    ])  # [E, 2*d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad]
   200→    gate_up_dq = gate_up_dq[:, :2 * d_expert, :d_hidden].to(torch.bfloat16)
   201→
   202→    down_dq = torch.stack([
   203→        _dequant_mxfp4(down_weight[e], down_weight_scale[e])
   204→        for e in range(E)
   205→    ])  # [E, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad]
   206→    down_dq = down_dq[:, :d_hidden, :d_expert].to(torch.bfloat16)
   207→
   208→    # Split gate_up -> gate [E, d_expert, d_hidden], up [E, d_expert, d_hidden]
   209→    gate_w, up_w = gate_up_dq.chunk(2, dim=1)  # each [E, d_expert, d_hidden]
   210→
   211→    # Per-token MoE forward
   212→    output = torch.zeros((M, d_hidden), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device=hidden_states.device)
   213→
   214→    for i in range(M):
   215→        x = hidden_states[i]  # [d_hidden]
   216→        for k in range(top_k):
   217→            eid = topk_ids[i, k].item()
   218→            w = topk_weights[i, k].item()
   219→
   220→            # Stage 1: gate_proj + up_proj + SwiGLU
   221→            gate_out = F.silu(x @ gate_w[eid].T)     # [d_expert]
   222→            up_out = x @ up_w[eid].T                  # [d_expert]
   223→            intermediate = gate_out * up_out           # [d_expert]
   224→
   225→            # Stage 2: down_proj
   226→            # down_dq[eid] is [d_hidden, d_expert], .T is [d_expert, d_hidden]
   227→            expert_out = intermediate @ down_dq[eid].T  # [d_hidden]
   228→
   229→            output[i] += w * expert_out
   230→
   231→    return output
   232→
   233→
   234→
   235→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   236→# ref_kernel: calls AITER fused_moe with MXFP4 quantized weights
   237→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   238→def ref_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
   239→    """
   240→    Reference implementation using AITER's fused_moe kernel with MXFP4 quantized weights.
   241→
   242→    Input data tuple (E = n_routed_experts + n_shared_experts, total_top_k = routed + shared):
   243→        hidden_states:                [M, d_hidden]                           bf16
   244→        gate_up_weight:               [E, 2*d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad//2]    fp4x2  (raw, before shuffle)
   245→        down_weight:                  [E, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad//2]      fp4x2  (raw, before shuffle)
   246→        gate_up_weight_scale:         [E, 2*d_expert_pad, scale_K]            e8m0   (raw, before shuffle)
   247→        down_weight_scale:            [E, d_hidden_pad, scale_K]              e8m0   (raw, before shuffle)
   248→        gate_up_weight_shuffled:      [E, 2*d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad//2]    fp4x2  (pre-shuffled)
   249→        down_weight_shuffled:         [E, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad//2]      fp4x2  (pre-shuffled)
   250→        gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled:[padded, flat]                          e8m0   (pre-shuffled)
   251→        down_weight_scale_shuffled:   [padded, flat]                          e8m0   (pre-shuffled)
   252→        topk_weights:                 [M, total_top_k]                        float32
   253→        topk_ids:                     [M, total_top_k]                        int32
   254→        config:                       dict
   255→
   256→    Returns:
   257→        output: [M, d_hidden] bf16
   258→    """
   259→    (
   260→        hidden_states,
   261→        gate_up_weight,
   262→        down_weight,
   263→        gate_up_weight_scale,
   264→        down_weight_scale,
   265→        gate_up_weight_shuffled,
   266→        down_weight_shuffled,
   267→        gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,
   268→        down_weight_scale_shuffled,
   269→        topk_weights,
   270→        topk_ids,
   271→        config,
   272→    ) = data
   273→
   274→    hidden_pad = config["d_hidden_pad"] - config["d_hidden"]
   275→    intermediate_pad = config["d_expert_pad"] - config["d_expert"]
   276→
   277→    output = fused_moe(
   278→        hidden_states,
   279→        gate_up_weight_shuffled,
   280→        down_weight_shuffled,
   281→        topk_weights,
   282→        topk_ids,
   283→        expert_mask=None,
   284→        activation=ActivationType.Silu,
   285→        quant_type=QuantType.per_1x32,  # MXFP4 uses per_1x32 block scaling
   286→        doweight_stage1=False,
   287→        w1_scale=gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,
   288→        w2_scale=down_weight_scale_shuffled,
   289→        a1_scale=None,
   290→        a2_scale=None,
   291→        hidden_pad=hidden_pad,
   292→        intermediate_pad=intermediate_pad,
   293→    )
   294→
   295→    return output
   296→
   297→
   298→
   299→check_implementation = make_match_reference(ref_kernel, rtol=5e-2, atol=5e-2)
   300→
```

Good — the skill confirms the JIT call-site issue and that `get_torch_quant` (pure PyTorch, no Triton JIT) is the key bypass. Now let me start implementing.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Why `get_torch_quant` bypasses the bug:** The root cause is that Triton JIT on AMD ROCm generates different GPU code depending on which Python module calls it. `get_torch_quant` uses pure PyTorch ops (no Triton JIT at all), so it's deterministic regardless of call-site. The reference's `generate_input` already uses `get_torch_quant` for MoE weight quantization (line 109 of `moe-mxfp4/reference.py`), proving it's a production-ready path.

**The `shuffle=True` question:** The GEMM reference uses `get_triton_quant(...)(A, shuffle=True)` which returns pre-shuffled quantized data + shuffled scales. `get_torch_quant` may not support the `shuffle` kwarg — if not, we need manual `shuffle_weight()` + `e8m0_shuffle()` calls.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Looking at the reference code, I can see:
- `ref_kernel` calls `get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)` then `quant_func(A, shuffle=True)` → returns `(A_q, A_scale_sh)` 
- Then passes to `gemm_a4w4(A_q, B_shuffle, A_scale_sh, B_scale_sh, dtype=bf16, bpreshuffle=True)`
- MoE reference uses `get_torch_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)` with `quant_dtype=dtypes.fp4x2` — different API

The `get_torch_quant` API differs: it takes `quant_dtype` as a kwarg instead of using `shuffle=True`. Let me write the GEMM submission using `get_torch_quant` with manual shuffle as the safe approach.

> TOOL

tool_use Write
id: toolu_013R94hmozLrnZZj3YFbBHY1
```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.worktrees/spec-luma-amd-speedrun/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"\nMXFP4 GEMM submission for AMD MI355X.\n\nUses get_torch_quant (pure PyTorch) instead of get_triton_quant to bypass\nthe Triton JIT call-site correctness bug on AMD ROCm. The torch quant path\nis deterministic regardless of which Python module calls it.\n\nB is already pre-quantized and shuffled in the input data, so we only need\nto quantize A, shuffle it, and call gemm_a4w4.\n\"\"\"\nimport torch\nimport aiter\nfrom aiter import QuantType, dtypes\nfrom aiter.ops.shuffle import shuffle_weight\nfrom aiter.utility import fp4_utils\nfrom task import input_t, output_t\n\n\ndef custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:\n    A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data\n    A = A.contiguous()\n\n    # Quantize A using get_torch_quant (no Triton JIT — bypasses call-site bug)\n    torch_quant = aiter.get_torch_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)\n    A_q, A_scale = torch_quant(A, quant_dtype=dtypes.fp4x2)\n\n    # Manual shuffle: tile-coalesced layout for gemm_a4w4\n    A_q = shuffle_weight(A_q, layout=(16, 16))\n    A_scale_sh = fp4_utils.e8m0_shuffle(A_scale)\n\n    out = aiter.gemm_a4w4(\n        A_q, B_shuffle, A_scale_sh, B_scale_sh,\n        dtype=dtypes.bf16, bpreshuffle=True,\n    )\n    return out\n"
}
```

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**`doweight_stage1` optimization:** In aiter's MoE kernel, token routing weights can be applied either in stage 1 (during the gate_up GEMM) or stage 2 (during the down GEMM). Applying in stage 1 means the intermediate activations are already weighted, reducing memory traffic in stage 2. For configs with many experts (E=257), this can save significant bandwidth because the intermediate tensor is larger than the hidden state.

**`block_size_M` tuning:** The MoE kernel tiles along the token dimension. The default auto-selection reads from CSV configs per GPU. Overriding to 64 or 128 can improve occupancy if the auto-selected value is suboptimal for our specific E/M/K shapes.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

This can improve GPU occupancy\nby converting part of the sparse workload into a dense one.\n\nFallback: If shared expert specialization is slower (e.g., for configs where\nthe shared expert is trivial), fall back to the vanilla fused_moe call.\n\"\"\"\nimport torch\nfrom task import input_t, output_t\nfrom aiter import ActivationType, QuantType\nfrom aiter.fused_moe import fused_moe\n\n\ndef custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:\n    (\n        hidden_states,\n        gate_up_weight,\n        down_weight,\n        gate_up_weight_scale,\n        down_weight_scale,\n        gate_up_weight_shuffled,\n        down_weight_shuffled,\n        gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,\n        down_weight_scale_shuffled,\n        topk_weights,\n        topk_ids,\n        config,\n    ) = data\n\n    hidden_pad = config[\"d_hidden_pad\"] - config[\"d_hidden\"]\n    intermediate_pad = config[\"d_expert_pad\"] - config[\"d_expert\"]\n\n    # Use unified fused_moe (same as reference — proven correct)\n    # Parameter exploration: try doweight_stage1=True to see if it helps\n    output = fused_moe(\n        hidden_states,\n        gate_up_weight_shuffled,\n        down_weight_shuffled,\n        topk_weights,\n        topk_ids,\n        expert_mask=None,\n        activation=ActivationType.Silu,\n        quant_type=QuantType.per_1x32,\n        doweight_stage1=False,\n        w1_scale=gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,\n        w2_scale=down_weight_scale_shuffled,\n        a1_scale=None,\n        a2_scale=None,\n        hidden_pad=hidden_pad,\n        intermediate_pad=intermediate_pad,\n    )\n\n    return output",

> AGENT

The error is clear: `shuffle_weight` requires the second-to-last dimension to be divisible by 16, but for small M (M=8 in this test case), the quantized A tensor has shape `[8, K//2]` — 8 is not divisible by 16.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Why the reference doesn't hit this:** `get_triton_quant(...)(A, shuffle=True)` does the shuffle *internally* as part of the quant kernel, handling padding. But `shuffle_weight` is a standalone op that requires the tensor to already be 16-aligned. For A (the activation, not the weight), small M values like 8 are common in GEMM benchmarks.

**The fix:** `gemm_a4w4` with `bpreshuffle=True` expects both operands to be pre-shuffled. But we can try `bpreshuffle=False` for A (meaning A is *not* pre-shuffled) — or we need to use `get_triton_quant` with `shuffle=True` which handles the padding internally. Actually, looking more carefully at the reference, `get_triton_quant(A, shuffle=True)` returns already-shuffled data. We need to find a way to get shuffled output from torch_quant, or try `get_hip_quant` instead.

**Alternative approach:** Try calling `get_torch_quant` *without* shuffle, then pass `bpreshuffle=False` to `gemm_a4w4` (if supported), or pad A to 16-align before shuffling.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Let me look at what `gemm_a4w4` accepts for `bpreshuffle` — the reference uses `bpreshuffle=True` meaning both inputs are pre-shuffled. Let me try two approaches:

1. **Pad A to 16-alignment before shuffle** (safest)
2. **Use `get_hip_quant`** which might handle shuffle internally like triton_quant

Let me go with approach 1 — pad M to a multiple of 16:

```
     1→"""
     2→Reference implementation for MLA (Multi-head Latent Attention) decode kernel.
     3→
     4→Uses aiter MLA kernels (mla_decode_fwd) as the reference.
     5→DeepSeek R1 forward_absorb MLA: absorbed q (576), compressed kv_buffer (576),
     6→output v_head_dim = kv_lora_rank = 512.
     7→
     8→The input provides:
     9→  q:       (total_q, num_heads, 576) bfloat16 — absorbed query (num_heads = 128 // tp)
    10→  kv_data: dict with KV cache in three formats:
    11→    "bf16":  Tensor  (total_kv, 1, 576)  bfloat16          — highest precision
    12→    "fp8":   (Tensor, Tensor)  kv_buffer fp8 + scalar scale — per-tensor quantized
    13→    "mxfp4": (Tensor, Tensor)  kv_buffer fp4x2 + fp8_e8m0  — block-32 quantized
    14→  The reference quantizes Q to fp8 on-the-fly inside ref_kernel.
    15→
    16→The reference kernel quantizes Q to fp8 on-the-fly and uses fp8 KV (a8w8 kernel),
    17→which is ~2-3x faster than bf16 on MI355X with negligible accuracy loss.
    18→
    19→Decode only — persistent mode with get_mla_metadata_v1.
    20→"""
    21→
    22→import torch
    23→import torch.nn.functional as F
    24→from task import input_t, output_t
    25→from utils import make_match_reference
    26→
    27→from aiter.mla import mla_decode_fwd
    28→from aiter import dtypes as aiter_dtypes
    29→from aiter import get_mla_metadata_info_v1, get_mla_metadata_v1
    30→from aiter.utility.fp4_utils import (
    31→    dynamic_mxfp4_quant,
    32→    mxfp4_to_f32,
    33→    e8m0_to_f32,
    34→)
    35→
    36→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    37→# DeepSeek R1 latent MQA constants (forward_absorb path)
    38→# https://huggingface.co/deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-R1-0528/blob/main/config.json
    39→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    40→TOTAL_NUM_HEADS = 128
    41→NUM_KV_HEADS = 1
    42→KV_LORA_RANK = 512
    43→QK_ROPE_HEAD_DIM = 64
    44→QK_HEAD_DIM = KV_LORA_RANK + QK_ROPE_HEAD_DIM   # 576
    45→V_HEAD_DIM = KV_LORA_RANK                        # 512
    46→SM_SCALE = 1.0 / (QK_HEAD_DIM ** 0.5)
    47→
    48→PAGE_SIZE = 1
    49→NUM_KV_SPLITS = 32
    50→
    51→# FP8 dtype (platform-specific via aiter)
    52→FP8_DTYPE = aiter_dtypes.fp8
    53→
    54→# Query dtype for the reference kernel: "fp8" or "bf16"
    55→Q_DTYPE = "fp8"
    56→
    57→# KV cache dtype for the reference kernel: "fp8" or "bf16"
    58→KV_DTYPE = "fp8"
    59→
    60→
    61→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    62→# FP8 quantization (sglang style: dynamic per-tensor)
    63→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    64→
    65→def quantize_fp8(tensor: torch.Tensor) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
    66→    """
    67→    Dynamic per-tensor FP8 quantization (following sglang scaled_fp8_quant).
    68→
    69→    Args:
    70→        tensor: bf16 tensor to quantize
    71→
    72→    Returns:
    73→        (fp8_tensor, scale) where scale is a scalar float32 tensor.
    74→        Dequantize: fp8_tensor.to(bf16) * scale
    75→    """
    76→    finfo = torch.finfo(FP8_DTYPE)
    77→    amax = tensor.abs().amax().clamp(min=1e-12)
    78→    scale = amax / finfo.max
    79→    fp8_tensor = (tensor / scale).clamp(min=finfo.min, max=finfo.max).to(FP8_DTYPE)
    80→    return fp8_tensor, scale.to(torch.float32).reshape(1)
    81→
    82→
    83→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    84→# MXFP4 quantization (aiter native: block-32, fp4x2 + fp8_e8m0 dtypes)
    85→# Uses aiter.utility.fp4_utils.dynamic_mxfp4_quant
    86→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    87→
    88→def quantize_mxfp4(tensor: torch.Tensor) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
    89→    """
    90→    MXFP4 block-wise quantization using aiter's dynamic_mxfp4_quant.
    91→
    92→    Block size = 32. Each block gets an E8M0 scale factor.
    93→    Two FP4 E2M1 values are packed per byte.
    94→
    95→    Args:
    96→        tensor: bf16 tensor of shape [B, M, N] (N must be divisible by 32)
    97→
    98→    Returns:
    99→        (fp4_data, scale_e8m0)
   100→        - fp4_data:   shape [B, M, N//2] in aiter_dtypes.fp4x2
   101→        - scale_e8m0: shape [B*M, ceil(N/32)] padded, in aiter_dtypes.fp8_e8m0
   102→    """
   103→    orig_shape = tensor.shape  # (B, M, N)
   104→    B, M, N = orig_shape
   105→
   106→    # dynamic_mxfp4_quant expects 2D: (B*M, N)
   107→    tensor_2d = tensor.reshape(B * M, N)
   108→    fp4_data_2d, scale_e8m0 = dynamic_mxfp4_quant(tensor_2d)
   109→
   110→    # Reshape fp4_data back to 3D: (B, M, N//2)
   111→    fp4_data = fp4_data_2d.view(B, M, N // 2)
   112→
   113→    return fp4_data, scale_e8m0
   114→
   115→
   116→def dequantize_mxfp4(
   117→    fp4_data: torch.Tensor,
   118→    scale_e8m0: torch.Tensor,
   119→    orig_shape: tuple,
   120→    dtype: torch.dtype = torch.bfloat16,
   121→) -> torch.Tensor:
   122→    """
   123→    Dequantize MXFP4 tensor using aiter utilities.
   124→
   125→    Note: dynamic_mxfp4_quant may pad both row and block dimensions in scale_e8m0.
   126→    We trim scales to match the actual data dimensions.
   127→
   128→    Args:
   129→        fp4_data:   packed FP4 data, shape [B, M, N//2] in fp4x2 or uint8
   130→        scale_e8m0: E8M0 block scale factors (possibly padded) in fp8_e8m0
   131→        orig_shape: original (B, M, N) for reshaping
   132→        dtype:      output dtype
   133→
   134→    Returns:
   135→        Dequantized tensor of shape orig_shape.
   136→    """
   137→    B, M, N = orig_shape
   138→    num_rows = B * M
   139→    block_size = 32
   140→    num_blocks = N // block_size  # actual blocks needed (e.g. 576/32 = 18)
   141→
   142→    # Unpack FP4 to float32: mxfp4_to_f32 expects (..., N//2) -> (..., N)
   143→    fp4_data_2d = fp4_data.reshape(num_rows, N // 2)
   144→    float_vals = mxfp4_to_f32(fp4_data_2d)  # (num_rows, N)
   145→
   146→    # Convert E8M0 scales to float32 and trim padded dimensions
   147→    scale_f32 = e8m0_to_f32(scale_e8m0)  # (padded_rows, padded_blocks)
   148→    scale_f32 = scale_f32[:num_rows, :num_blocks]  # (num_rows, num_blocks)
   149→
   150→    # Apply block scales
   151→    float_vals_blocked = float_vals.view(num_rows, num_blocks, block_size)
   152→    scaled = float_vals_blocked * scale_f32.unsqueeze(-1)
   153→
   154→    return scaled.view(B, M, N).to(dtype)
   155→
   156→
   157→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   158→# Persistent mode metadata helpers
   159→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   160→
   161→def _make_mla_decode_metadata(
   162→    batch_size: int,
   163→    max_q_len: int,
   164→    nhead: int,
   165→    nhead_kv: int,
   166→    q_dtype: torch.dtype,
   167→    kv_dtype: torch.dtype,
   168→    qo_indptr: torch.Tensor,
   169→    kv_indptr: torch.Tensor,
   170→    kv_last_page_len: torch.Tensor,
   171→    num_kv_splits: int = NUM_KV_SPLITS,
   172→):
   173→    """Allocate and populate work buffers for persistent mla_decode_fwd."""
   174→    info = get_mla_metadata_info_v1(
   175→        batch_size, max_q_len, nhead, q_dtype, kv_dtype,
   176→        is_sparse=False, fast_mode=False,
   177→        num_kv_splits=num_kv_splits, intra_batch_mode=True,
   178→    )
   179→    work = [torch.empty(s, dtype=t, device="cuda") for s, t in info]
   180→    (work_metadata, work_indptr, work_info_set,
   181→     reduce_indptr, reduce_final_map, reduce_partial_map) = work
   182→
   183→    # Populate the metadata buffers
   184→    get_mla_metadata_v1(
   185→        qo_indptr, kv_indptr, kv_last_page_len,
   186→        nhead // nhead_kv,   # num_heads_per_head_k
   187→        nhead_kv,            # num_heads_k
   188→        True,                # is_causal
   189→        work_metadata, work_info_set, work_indptr,
   190→        reduce_indptr, reduce_final_map, reduce_partial_map,
   191→        page_size=PAGE_SIZE,
   192→        kv_granularity=max(PAGE_SIZE, 16),
   193→        max_seqlen_qo=max_q_len,
   194→        uni_seqlen_qo=max_q_len,
   195→        fast_mode=False,
   196→        max_split_per_batch=num_kv_splits,
   197→        intra_batch_mode=True,
   198→        dtype_q=q_dtype,
   199→        dtype_kv=kv_dtype,
   200→    )
   201→
   202→    return {
   203→        "work_meta_data": work_metadata,
   204→        "work_indptr": work_indptr,
   205→        "work_info_set": work_info_set,
   206→        "reduce_indptr": reduce_indptr,
   207→        "reduce_final_map": reduce_final_map,
   208→        "reduce_partial_map": reduce_partial_map,
   209→    }
   210→
   211→
   212→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   213→# Aiter reference kernel (decode only)
   214→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   215→
   216→def _aiter_mla_decode(
   217→    q: torch.Tensor,
   218→    kv_buffer: torch.Tensor,
   219→    qo_indptr: torch.Tensor,
   220→    kv_indptr: torch.Tensor,
   221→    config: dict,
   222→    q_scale: torch.Tensor | None = None,
   223→    kv_scale: torch.Tensor | None = None,
   224→) -> torch.Tensor:
   225→    """
   226→    MLA decode attention using aiter persistent-mode kernel.
   227→
   228→    Supports multiple Q/KV dtype combinations:
   229→      - Q_DTYPE="fp8":  fp8 Q + fp8 KV (a8w8) — fastest on MI355X
   230→      - Q_DTYPE="bf16": bf16 Q + bf16 KV (a16w16) — highest precision
   231→
   232→    q:          (total_q, num_heads, 576)  fp8 or bf16
   233→    kv_buffer:  (total_kv, 1, 576)         fp8 or bf16
   234→    q_scale:    scalar float32 (required for fp8 Q, None for bf16)
   235→    kv_scale:   scalar float32 (required for fp8 KV, None for bf16)
   236→    """
   237→    batch_size = config["batch_size"]
   238→    nq = config["num_heads"]
   239→    nkv = config["num_kv_heads"]
   240→    dq = config["qk_head_dim"]
   241→    dv = config["v_head_dim"]
   242→    q_seq_len = config["q_seq_len"]
   243→
   244→    total_kv_len = int(kv_indptr[-1].item())
   245→    kv_indices = torch.arange(total_kv_len, dtype=torch.int32, device="cuda")
   246→
   247→    # Reshape kv_buffer to 4D for aiter: (total_kv, page_size, nhead_kv, dim)
   248→    kv_buffer_4d = kv_buffer.view(kv_buffer.shape[0], PAGE_SIZE, nkv, kv_buffer.shape[-1])
   249→
   250→    max_q_len = q_seq_len
   251→    kv_last_page_len = (kv_indptr[1:] - kv_indptr[:-1]).to(torch.int32)
   252→
   253→    # Build persistent-mode metadata
   254→    meta = _make_mla_decode_metadata(
   255→        batch_size, max_q_len, nq, nkv,
   256→        q.dtype, kv_buffer.dtype,
   257→        qo_indptr, kv_indptr, kv_last_page_len,
   258→        num_kv_splits=NUM_KV_SPLITS,
   259→    )
   260→
   261→    o = torch.empty((q.shape[0], nq, dv), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda")
   262→    mla_decode_fwd(
   263→        q.view(-1, nq, dq),
   264→        kv_buffer_4d,
   265→        o,
   266→        qo_indptr,
   267→        kv_indptr,
   268→        kv_indices,
   269→        kv_last_page_len,
   270→        max_q_len,
   271→        page_size=PAGE_SIZE,
   272→        nhead_kv=nkv,
   273→        sm_scale=SM_SCALE,
   274→        logit_cap=0.0,
   275→        num_kv_splits=NUM_KV_SPLITS,
   276→        q_scale=q_scale,
   277→        kv_scale=kv_scale,
   278→        intra_batch_mode=True,
   279→        **meta,
   280→    )
   281→    return o
   282→
   283→
   284→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   285→# generate_input / ref_kernel / check_implementation
   286→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   287→
   288→def generate_input(batchsize: int, qseqlen: int, kvseqlen: int, tp: int, seed: int) -> input_t:
   289→    """
   290→    Generate absorbed q and compressed kv_buffer for MLA decode.
   291→
   292→    Args:
   293→        tp: tensor parallelism degree (4 or 8). num_heads = TOTAL_NUM_HEADS // tp.
   294→
   295→    Returns all three KV cache formats in kv_data dict:
   296→      kv_data = {
   297→        "bf16":  Tensor               — (total_kv, 1, 576) bfloat16
   298→        "fp8":   (Tensor, Tensor)     — kv_buffer fp8 + scalar scale
   299→        "mxfp4": (Tensor, Tensor)     — kv_buffer fp4x2 + fp8_e8m0 scale
   300→      }
   301→    """
   302→    assert TOTAL_NUM_HEADS % tp == 0, f"TOTAL_NUM_HEADS ({TOTAL_NUM_HEADS}) must be divisible by tp ({tp})"
   303→    num_heads = TOTAL_NUM_HEADS // tp
   304→
   305→    gen = torch.Generator(device="cuda")
   306→    gen.manual_seed(seed)
   307→
   308→    total_q = batchsize * qseqlen
   309→    total_kv = batchsize * kvseqlen
   310→
   311→    # Absorbed query: (total_q, num_heads, 576) bf16
   312→    q = torch.randn(
   313→        (total_q, num_heads, QK_HEAD_DIM),
   314→        dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda", generator=gen,
   315→    ) * 0.02
   316→
   317→    # Compressed KV buffer: (total_kv, 1, 576) bf16 — the source of truth
   318→    kv_buffer_bf16 = torch.randn(
   319→        (total_kv, NUM_KV_HEADS, QK_HEAD_DIM),
   320→        dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda", generator=gen,
   321→    ) * 0.02
   322→
   323→    # Quantize KV to fp8
   324→    kv_buffer_fp8, kv_scale_fp8 = quantize_fp8(kv_buffer_bf16)
   325→
   326→    # Quantize KV to mxfp4
   327→    kv_buffer_mxfp4, kv_scale_mxfp4 = quantize_mxfp4(kv_buffer_bf16)
   328→
   329→    # All three KV formats: bf16 is a Tensor, fp8/mxfp4 are (Tensor, Tensor) tuples
   330→    kv_data = {
   331→        "bf16": kv_buffer_bf16,
   332→        "fp8": (kv_buffer_fp8, kv_scale_fp8),
   333→        "mxfp4": (kv_buffer_mxfp4, kv_scale_mxfp4),
   334→    }
   335→
   336→    qo_indptr = torch.arange(0, batchsize + 1, dtype=torch.int32, device="cuda") * qseqlen
   337→    kv_indptr = torch.arange(0, batchsize + 1, dtype=torch.int32, device="cuda") * kvseqlen
   338→
   339→    config = {
   340→        "batch_size": batchsize,
   341→        "num_heads": num_heads,
   342→        "num_kv_heads": NUM_KV_HEADS,
   343→        "qk_head_dim": QK_HEAD_DIM,
   344→        "kv_lora_rank": KV_LORA_RANK,
   345→        "qk_rope_head_dim": QK_ROPE_HEAD_DIM,
   346→        "v_head_dim": V_HEAD_DIM,
   347→        "q_seq_len": qseqlen,
   348→        "kv_seq_len": kvseqlen,
   349→        "sm_scale": SM_SCALE,
   350→    }
   351→
   352→    return (q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config)
   353→
   354→
   355→def ref_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
   356→    """Reference MLA decode attention. Uses Q_DTYPE and KV_DTYPE to select kernel variant."""
   357→    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config = data
   358→
   359→    # Resolve Q
   360→    if Q_DTYPE == "fp8":
   361→        q_input, q_scale = quantize_fp8(q)
   362→    else:
   363→        q_input, q_scale = q, None
   364→
   365→    # Resolve KV
   366→    if KV_DTYPE == "fp8":
   367→        kv_buffer_fp8, kv_scale = kv_data["fp8"]
   368→        kv_input = kv_buffer_fp8
   369→    else:
   370→        kv_input, kv_scale = kv_data["bf16"], None
   371→
   372→    return _aiter_mla_decode(
   373→        q_input, kv_input, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config,
   374→        q_scale=q_scale, kv_scale=kv_scale,
   375→    )
   376→
   377→
   378→check_implementation = make_match_reference(ref_kernel, rtol=1e-02, atol=1e-02)
   379→
```

Good. Now let me prepare the MLA optimization. The key finding from the research is that the auto-tuner uses much lower `NUM_KV_SPLITS` (1-16 instead of 32) and `fast_mode=True` for smaller metadata buffers. Let me implement batch-size-dependent NUM_KV_SPLITS with fast_mode:

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.worktrees/spec-luma-amd-speedrun/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/kernels/mixed-mla/submission.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"\nMLA decode kernel optimized for AMD MI355X.\n\nOptimizations over reference:\n1. Batch-size-dependent NUM_KV_SPLITS (auto-tuner uses 1-16, not fixed 32)\n2. fast_mode=True for smaller metadata work buffers\n\"\"\"\nimport torch\nfrom task import input_t, output_t\nfrom aiter import dtypes as aiter_dtypes\nfrom aiter.mla import mla_decode_fwd\nfrom aiter import get_mla_metadata_info_v1, get_mla_metadata_v1\n\nFP8_DTYPE = aiter_dtypes.fp8\n\n# MLA constants (DeepSeek R1 forward_absorb)\nNUM_KV_HEADS = 1\nKV_LORA_RANK = 512\nQK_HEAD_DIM = 576\nV_HEAD_DIM = KV_LORA_RANK       # 512\nSM_SCALE = 1.0 / (QK_HEAD_DIM ** 0.5)\nPAGE_SIZE = 1\n\n\ndef quantize_fp8(tensor: torch.Tensor) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:\n    finfo = torch.finfo(FP8_DTYPE)\n    amax = tensor.abs().amax().clamp(min=1e-12)\n    scale = amax / finfo.max\n    fp8_tensor = (tensor / scale).clamp(min=finfo.min, max=finfo.max).to(FP8_DTYPE)\n    return fp8_tensor, scale.to(torch.float32).reshape(1)\n\n\ndef _pick_num_kv_splits(batch_size: int, nhead: int, total_kv: int) -> int:\n    \"\"\"Pick NUM_KV_SPLITS based on workload size, matching aiter auto-tuner logic.\"\"\"\n    # aiter auto-tuner picks from 1-16 based on gpu_util * compute_efficiency\n    # For FP8 with nhead*max_seqlen_q as key: {16:128, 32:128, 64:64, 128:32}\n    tokens = nhead * batch_size  # proxy for nhead * max_seqlen_q\n    if tokens <= 16:\n        return 16\n    elif tokens <= 32:\n        return 16\n    elif tokens <= 64:\n        return 8\n    elif tokens <= 128:\n        return 8\n    else:\n        return 16\n\n\ndef _build_metadata(\n    batch_size: int,\n    max_q_len: int,\n    nhead: int,\n    nhead_kv: int,\n    q_dtype: torch.dtype,\n    kv_dtype: torch.dtype,\n    qo_indptr: torch.Tensor,\n    kv_indptr: torch.Tensor,\n    kv_last_page_len: torch.Tensor,\n    num_kv_splits: int,\n):\n    info = get_mla_metadata_info_v1(\n        batch_size, max_q_len, nhead, q_dtype, kv_dtype,\n        is_sparse=False, fast_mode=True,\n        num_kv_splits=num_kv_splits, intra_batch_mode=True,\n    )\n    work = [torch.empty(s, dtype=t, device=\"cuda\") for s, t in info]\n    (work_metadata, work_indptr, work_info_set,\n     reduce_indptr, reduce_final_map, reduce_partial_map) = work\n\n    get_mla_metadata_v1(\n        qo_indptr, kv_indptr, kv_last_page_len,\n        nhead // nhead_kv,\n        nhead_kv,\n        True,  # is_causal\n        work_metadata, work_info_set, work_indptr,\n        reduce_indptr, reduce_final_map, reduce_partial_map,\n        page_size=PAGE_SIZE,\n        kv_granularity=max(PAGE_SIZE, 16),\n        max_seqlen_qo=max_q_len,\n        uni_seqlen_qo=max_q_len,\n        fast_mode=True,\n        max_split_per_batch=num_kv_splits,\n        intra_batch_mode=True,\n        dtype_q=q_dtype,\n        dtype_kv=kv_dtype,\n    )\n\n    return {\n        \"work_meta_data\": work_metadata,\n        \"work_indptr\": work_indptr,\n        \"work_info_set\": work_info_set,\n        \"reduce_indptr\": reduce_indptr,\n        \"reduce_final_map\": reduce_final_map,\n        \"reduce_partial_map\": reduce_partial_map,\n    }\n\n\ndef custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:\n    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config = data\n\n    batch_size = config[\"batch_size\"]\n    nq = config[\"num_heads\"]\n    nkv = config[\"num_kv_heads\"]\n    dq = config[\"qk_head_dim\"]\n    dv = config[\"v_head_dim\"]\n    q_seq_len = config[\"q_seq_len\"]\n\n    # Quantize Q to fp8\n    q_fp8, q_scale = quantize_fp8(q)\n\n    # Use fp8 KV cache (pre-quantized in generate_input)\n    kv_buffer_fp8, kv_scale_fp8 = kv_data[\"fp8\"]\n\n    # Build KV indices (contiguous pages)\n    total_kv_len = int(kv_indptr[-1].item())\n    kv_indices = torch.arange(total_kv_len, dtype=torch.int32, device=\"cuda\")\n\n    # Reshape kv_buffer to 4D: (total_kv, page_size, nhead_kv, dim)\n    kv_4d = kv_buffer_fp8.view(\n        kv_buffer_fp8.shape[0], PAGE_SIZE, nkv, kv_buffer_fp8.shape[-1]\n    )\n\n    max_q_len = q_seq_len\n    kv_last_page_len = (kv_indptr[1:] - kv_indptr[:-1]).to(torch.int32)\n\n    # Pick optimal NUM_KV_SPLITS based on batch config\n    num_splits = _pick_num_kv_splits(batch_size, nq, total_kv_len)\n\n    # Build persistent-mode metadata with fast_mode=True\n    meta = _build_metadata(\n        batch_size, max_q_len, nq, nkv,\n        q_fp8.dtype, kv_buffer_fp8.dtype,\n        qo_indptr, kv_indptr, kv_last_page_len,\n        num_splits,\n    )\n\n    # Allocate output\n    o = torch.empty((q.shape[0], nq, dv), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device=\"cuda\")\n\n    # Run aiter persistent-mode MLA decode\n    mla_decode_fwd(\n        q_fp8.view(-1, nq, dq),\n        kv_4d,\n        o,\n        qo_indptr,\n        kv_indptr,\n        kv_indices,\n        kv_last_page_len,\n        max_q_len,\n        page_size=PAGE_SIZE,\n        nhead_kv=nkv,\n        sm_scale=SM_SCALE,\n        logit_cap=0.0,\n        num_kv_splits=num_splits,\n        q_scale=q_scale,\n        kv_scale=kv_scale_fp8,\n        intra_batch_mode=True,\n        **meta,\n    )\n\n    return o\n"
}
```

The server is very backed up — over 13 minutes waiting. While we wait, let me think about an alternative GEMM approach. The `shuffle_weight` assertion failure for small M is a real problem. Let me also consider whether `get_triton_quant` can be used from *within* the reference module (by importing and calling it indirectly). Actually, there's another approach — look at what `get_torch_quant` actually returns and whether `gemm_a4w4` can work with unshuffled A:

Looking at the reference code more carefully:
- `aiter.gemm_a4w4(A_q, B_shuffle, A_scale_sh, B_scale_sh, dtype=bf16, bpreshuffle=True)` — both inputs are pre-shuffled
- The `bpreshuffle` parameter likely controls whether B is pre-shuffled, not A

```
     2→MXFP4 GEMM submission for AMD MI355X.

     9→import aiter
    10→from aiter import QuantType, dtypes
    11→from aiter.ops.shuffle import shuffle_weight
    12→from aiter.utility import fp4_utils

    15→

    18→    A = A.contiguous()

    21→    # Pad M to multiple of 16 for shuffle_weight compatibility
    22→    pad_m = (16 - m % 16) % 16
    23→    if pad_m > 0:
    24→        A = torch.nn.functional.pad(A, (0, 0, 0, pad_m))
    25→

    27→    torch_quant = aiter.get_torch_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
    28→    A_q, A_scale = torch_quant(A, quant_dtype=dtypes.fp4x2)

    36→        dtype=dtypes.bf16, bpreshuffle=True,
    37→    )
```

The current approach pads M to 16-align. Let me also think about a hybrid approach — for small M that doesn't divide by 16, we could try `get_hip_quant` which might handle it internally. But actually, let me also consider an important alternative: use `get_triton_quant` but call it from the reference module's scope. We could import it from there. But wait — the skill says even module-level caching doesn't work. The call-site is what matters.

Let me also think about this differently — `get_torch_quant` returns raw quantized data (no shuffle). What if we use `gemm_a4w4` with `bpreshuffle=False`? The question is: what does `bpreshuffle` actually control? Looking at the reference comments: "gemm_a4w4 needs shuffled B_q and shuffled scales (see test_gemm_a4w4.py:102-105)".

Actually, `bpreshuffle=True` tells `gemm_a4w4` that the *weight (B)* is already pre-shuffled — it doesn't necessarily say A must be shuffled too. Let me try without shuffling A:

> TOOL

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.worktrees/spec-luma-amd-speedrun/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"\nMXFP4 GEMM submission for AMD MI355X.\n\nUses get_torch_quant (pure PyTorch) instead of get_triton_quant to bypass\nthe Triton JIT call-site correctness bug on AMD ROCm. Pads A's M dimension\nto 16-alignment for shuffle_weight compatibility.\n\"\"\"\nimport torch\nimport aiter\nfrom aiter import QuantType, dtypes\nfrom aiter.ops.shuffle import shuffle_weight\nfrom aiter.utility import fp4_utils\nfrom task import input_t, output_t\n\n# Cache quant function at module level\n_torch_quant = aiter.get_torch_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)\n\n\ndef custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:\n    A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data\n    A = A.contiguous()\n    m, k = A.shape\n\n    # Pad M to multiple of 16 for shuffle_weight compatibility\n    m_padded = (m + 15) // 16 * 16\n    if m_padded != m:\n        A = torch.nn.functional.pad(A, (0, 0, 0, m_padded - m))\n\n    # Quantize A using get_torch_quant (no Triton JIT — bypasses call-site bug)\n    A_q, A_scale = _torch_quant(A, quant_dtype=dtypes.fp4x2)\n\n    # Manual shuffle: tile-coalesced layout for gemm_a4w4\n    A_q = shuffle_weight(A_q, layout=(16, 16))\n    A_scale_sh = fp4_utils.e8m0_shuffle(A_scale)\n\n    out = aiter.gemm_a4w4(\n        A_q, B_shuffle, A_scale_sh, B_scale_sh,\n        dtype=dtypes.bf16, bpreshuffle=True,\n    )\n    return out[:m]\n"
}
```

> TOOL

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.worktrees/spec-luma-amd-speedrun/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py"
```

id: toolu_01PurfMmJbW8GWZSk2bAPTYM
```
     1→"""
     2→MXFP4 GEMM submission for AMD MI355X.
     3→
     4→Uses get_torch_quant (pure PyTorch) instead of get_triton_quant to bypass
     5→the Triton JIT call-site correctness bug on AMD ROCm. Pads A's M dimension
     6→to 16-alignment for shuffle_weight compatibility.
     7→"""
     8→import torch
     9→import aiter
    10→from aiter import QuantType, dtypes
    11→from aiter.ops.shuffle import shuffle_weight
    12→from aiter.utility import fp4_utils
    13→from task import input_t, output_t
    14→
    15→# Cache quant function at module level
    16→_torch_quant = aiter.get_torch_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
    17→
    18→
    19→def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    20→    A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data
    21→    A = A.contiguous()
    22→    m, k = A.shape
    23→
    24→    # Pad M to multiple of 16 for shuffle_weight compatibility
    25→    m_padded = (m + 15) // 16 * 16
    26→    if m_padded != m:
    27→        A = torch.nn.functional.pad(A, (0, 0, 0, m_padded - m))
    28→
    29→    # Quantize A using get_torch_quant (no Triton JIT — bypasses call-site bug)
    30→    A_q, A_scale = _torch_quant(A, quant_dtype=dtypes.fp4x2)
    31→
    32→    # Manual shuffle: tile-coalesced layout for gemm_a4w4
    33→    A_q = shuffle_weight(A_q, layout=(16, 16))
    34→    A_scale_sh = fp4_utils.e8m0_shuffle(A_scale)
    35→
    36→    out = aiter.gemm_a4w4(
    37→        A_q, B_shuffle, A_scale_sh, B_scale_sh,
    38→        dtype=dtypes.bf16, bpreshuffle=True,
    39→    )
    40→    return out[:m]
    41→
```

> AGENT

The GEMM submission ran successfully this time (the queue cleared) but **all 4 tests failed with massive mismatches**. The `get_torch_quant` + manual `shuffle_weight` + `e8m0_shuffle` approach is producing completely wrong results. The errors are huge (not 1-3% drift — completely wrong values).

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Why the manual shuffle fails:** The `get_torch_quant` quant function returns data in a different format than what `shuffle_weight` expects. `get_triton_quant(A, shuffle=True)` does the quantization AND shuffle as a single coordinated operation — the shuffle is applied to the already-quantized data in a way that matches `gemm_a4w4`'s expected input layout. When we do `get_torch_quant` followed by separate `shuffle_weight`, the shuffle is being applied to data in the wrong format (torch_quant's output format differs from triton_quant's).

**The real insight from the MoE reference:** The MoE `generate_input` (line 109-118) uses `get_torch_quant` → `shuffle_weight` → `e8m0_shuffle` successfully for **B (weights)**, not for A (activations). The key difference: for weights, the quant is done once during data generation. But for A, the padding behavior differs between torch_quant and triton_quant.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

This means the `get_torch_quant` path doesn't produce compatible output for `gemm_a4w4` when used for the activation (A) side. The delegation to `ref_kernel` remains the only correct approach. Let me revert to the reference delegation approach — which we know is correct — and focus on the MoE and MLA kernels where we can actually make gains.

> AGENT

MoE with `doweight_stage1=True` **failed correctness**. Looking at the stderr logs closely:

The kernel builds TWO versions — one with `mulWeightStage1_` and one with `mulWeightStage2_` — and runs both (the reference with `doweight_stage1=False` and our submission with `doweight_stage1=True`). The errors are ~10-30% magnitude differences (e.g., 0.369 vs 0.404), which exceed the rtol=5e-2 tolerance.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Why `doweight_stage1=True` fails correctness:** `doweight_stage1` changes *when* the topk_weights are multiplied — in stage 1 (gate_up GEMM) vs stage 2 (down GEMM). Mathematically, `w * (SiLU(gate(x)) * up(x)) @ down` vs `SiLU(gate(w*x)) * up(w*x) @ down` — the SiLU activation is nonlinear, so multiplying the weight before vs after the activation produces different results. The reference uses `doweight_stage1=False`, so the check_implementation compares against that. This isn't a performance issue — it's a correctness mismatch because the two approaches compute different values.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

I need to revert MoE to `doweight_stage1=False` (matching reference) and try the other optimization parameters instead. Let me try `block_size_M` override:

```json
  "content": "\"\"\"\nMXFP4 MoE submission for AMD MI355X.\n\nUses the reference fused_moe call with block_size_M=64 override to try\nimproving GPU occupancy for small-batch configs where the auto-tuner\npicks block_m=32.\n\nThe sorting and dispatch parameters are kept at defaults (doweight_stage1=False)\nto match reference correctness.\n\"\"\"\nfrom task import input_t, output_t\nfrom aiter import ActivationType, QuantType\nfrom aiter.fused_moe import fused_moe\n\n\ndef custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:\n    (\n        hidden_states,\n        gate_up_weight,\n        down_weight,\n        gate_up_weight_scale,\n        down_weight_scale,\n        gate_up_weight_shuffled,\n        down_weight_shuffled,\n        gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,\n        down_weight_scale_shuffled,\n        topk_weights,\n        topk_ids,\n        config,\n    ) = data\n\n    hidden_pad = config[\"d_hidden_pad\"] - config[\"d_hidden\"]\n    intermediate_pad = config[\"d_expert_pad\"] - config[\"d_expert\"]\n\n    output = fused_moe(\n        hidden_states,\n        gate_up_weight_shuffled,\n        down_weight_shuffled,\n        topk_weights,\n        topk_ids,\n        expert_mask=None,\n        activation=ActivationType.Silu,\n        quant_type=QuantType.per_1x32,\n        doweight_stage1=False,\n        block_size_M=64,\n        w1_scale=gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,\n        w2_scale=down_weight_scale_shuffled,\n        a1_scale=None,\n        a2_scale=None,\n        hidden_pad=hidden_pad,\n        intermediate_pad=intermediate_pad,\n    )\n\n    return output\n"
```

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**The `fast_mode=True` + adaptive splits made things SLOWER (224 us vs 191 us).** Our adaptive `_pick_num_kv_splits` function was too aggressive in reducing splits — using 8 or 16 instead of 32. For large KV sequences (kv=8192, bs=256), the kernel needs more splits to parallelize the KV processing. The reference's fixed NUM_KV_SPLITS=32 is actually close to optimal for the benchmark configs.

**Key lesson:** The aiter auto-tuner uses lower splits for *different shapes* than what the benchmark tests. The benchmark specifically tests large KV sequences where more splits help.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Let me go back to NUM_KV_SPLITS=32 but keep `fast_mode=True` to see if just the smaller metadata buffers help:

> TOOL

tool_use Write
id: toolu_01G2aX3UwXt93fFrob95fFgc
```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.worktrees/spec-luma-amd-speedrun/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/kernels/mixed-mla/submission.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"\nMLA decode kernel optimized for AMD MI355X.\n\nOptimization over reference: fast_mode=True for smaller metadata work buffers.\nKeeps NUM_KV_SPLITS=32 (matching reference) — adaptive splits were slower\nbecause the benchmark configs use large KV sequences where more splits help.\n\"\"\"\nimport torch\nfrom task import input_t, output_t\nfrom aiter import dtypes as aiter_dtypes\nfrom aiter.mla import mla_decode_fwd\nfrom aiter import get_mla_metadata_info_v1, get_mla_metadata_v1\n\nFP8_DTYPE = aiter_dtypes.fp8\n\n# MLA constants (DeepSeek R1 forward_absorb)\nNUM_KV_HEADS = 1\nKV_LORA_RANK = 512\nQK_HEAD_DIM = 576\nV_HEAD_DIM = KV_LORA_RANK       # 512\nSM_SCALE = 1.0 / (QK_HEAD_DIM ** 0.5)\nPAGE_SIZE = 1\nNUM_KV_SPLITS = 32\n\n\ndef quantize_fp8(tensor: torch.Tensor) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:\n    finfo = torch.finfo(FP8_DTYPE)\n    amax = tensor.abs().amax().clamp(min=1e-12)\n    scale = amax / finfo.max\n    fp8_tensor = (tensor / scale).clamp(min=finfo.min, max=finfo.max).to(FP8_DTYPE)\n    return fp8_tensor, scale.to(torch.float32).reshape(1)\n\n\ndef _build_metadata(\n    batch_size: int,\n    max_q_len: int,\n    nhead: int,\n    nhead_kv: int,\n    q_dtype: torch.dtype,\n    kv_dtype: torch.dtype,\n    qo_indptr: torch.Tensor,\n    kv_indptr: torch.Tensor,\n    kv_last_page_len: torch.Tensor,\n):\n    info = get_mla_metadata_info_v1(\n        batch_size, max_q_len, nhead, q_dtype, kv_dtype,\n        is_sparse=False, fast_mode=True,\n        num_kv_splits=NUM_KV_SPLITS, intra_batch_mode=True,\n    )\n    work = [torch.empty(s, dtype=t, device=\"cuda\") for s, t in info]\n    (work_metadata, work_indptr, work_info_set,\n     reduce_indptr, reduce_final_map, reduce_partial_map) = work\n\n    get_mla_metadata_v1(\n        qo_indptr, kv_indptr, kv_last_page_len,\n        nhead // nhead_kv,\n        nhead_kv,\n        True,  # is_causal\n        work_metadata, work_info_set, work_indptr,\n        reduce_indptr, reduce_final_map, reduce_partial_map,\n        page_size=PAGE_SIZE,\n        kv_granularity=max(PAGE_SIZE, 16),\n        max_seqlen_qo=max_q_len,\n        uni_seqlen_qo=max_q_len,\n        fast_mode=True,\n        max_split_per_batch=NUM_KV_SPLITS,\n        intra_batch_mode=True,\n        dtype_q=q_dtype,\n        dtype_kv=kv_dtype,\n    )\n\n    return {\n        \"work_meta_data\": work_metadata,\n        \"work_indptr\": work_indptr,\n        \"work_info_set\": work_info_set,\n        \"reduce_indptr\": reduce_indptr,\n        \"reduce_final_map\": reduce_final_map,\n        \"reduce_partial_map\": reduce_partial_map,\n    }\n\n\ndef custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:\n    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config = data\n\n    batch_size = config[\"batch_size\"]\n    nq = config[\"num_heads\"]\n    nkv = config[\"num_kv_heads\"]\n    dq = config[\"qk_head_dim\"]\n    dv = config[\"v_head_dim\"]\n    q_seq_len = config[\"q_seq_len\"]\n\n    # Quantize Q to fp8\n    q_fp8, q_scale = quantize_fp8(q)\n\n    # Use fp8 KV cache (pre-quantized in generate_input)\n    kv_buffer_fp8, kv_scale_fp8 = kv_data[\"fp8\"]\n\n    # Build KV indices (contiguous pages)\n    total_kv_len = int(kv_indptr[-1].item())\n    kv_indices = torch.arange(total_kv_len, dtype=torch.int32, device=\"cuda\")\n\n    # Reshape kv_buffer to 4D: (total_kv, page_size, nhead_kv, dim)\n    kv_4d = kv_buffer_fp8.view(\n        kv_buffer_fp8.shape[0], PAGE_SIZE, nkv, kv_buffer_fp8.shape[-1]\n    )\n\n    max_q_len = q_seq_len\n    kv_last_page_len = (kv_indptr[1:] - kv_indptr[:-1]).to(torch.int32)\n\n    # Build persistent-mode metadata with fast_mode=True\n    meta = _build_metadata(\n        batch_size, max_q_len, nq, nkv,\n        q_fp8.dtype, kv_buffer_fp8.dtype,\n        qo_indptr, kv_indptr, kv_last_page_len,\n    )\n\n    # Allocate output\n    o = torch.empty((q.shape[0], nq, dv), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device=\"cuda\")\n\n    # Run aiter persistent-mode MLA decode\n    mla_decode_fwd(\n        q_fp8.view(-1, nq, dq),\n        kv_4d,\n        o,\n        qo_indptr,\n        kv_indptr,\n        kv_indices,\n        kv_last_page_len,\n        max_q_len,\n        page_size=PAGE_SIZE,\n        nhead_kv=nkv,\n        sm_scale=SM_SCALE,\n        logit_cap=0.0,\n        num_kv_splits=NUM_KV_SPLITS,\n        q_scale=q_scale,\n        kv_scale=kv_scale_fp8,\n        intra_batch_mode=True,\n        **meta,\n    )\n\n    return o\n"
}
```

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Both MLA optimizations are slower than the reference (191 us).** The `fast_mode=True` flag actually increases latency by ~21% despite using smaller metadata buffers. This likely means the `fast_mode` path has different internal dispatch logic that's suboptimal for these shapes.

**The reference is already well-tuned.** The reference at 191 us was likely already using the optimal path. Our changes to `fast_mode` just switched to a different (slower) code path in the aiter kernel. The reference uses `fast_mode=False` which seems to be the better choice for MI355X.

**Conclusion for MLA:** The best approach is to match the reference exactly. The 44x gap to the leader (4.3 us) comes from using custom ASM kernels — not from parameter tuning of the same `mla_decode_fwd` API.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Let me revert MLA to match the reference exactly and submit for leaderboard — at least it'll maintain our current position:

```json
{

  "content": "\"\"\"\nMLA decode kernel for AMD MI355X.\n\nUses aiter's mla_decode_fwd persistent-mode kernel with fp8 Q + fp8 KV.\nMatches reference parameters exactly — fast_mode and adaptive splits\nboth tested slower than the reference defaults.\n\"\"\"\nimport torch\nfrom task import input_t, output_t\nfrom aiter import dtypes as aiter_dtypes\nfrom aiter.mla import mla_decode_fwd\nfrom aiter import get_mla_metadata_info_v1, get_mla_metadata_v1\n\nFP8_DTYPE = aiter_dtypes.fp8\n\n# MLA constants (DeepSeek R1 forward_absorb)\nNUM_KV_HEADS = 1\nKV_LORA_RANK = 512\nQK_HEAD_DIM = 576\nV_HEAD_DIM = KV_LORA_RANK       # 512\nSM_SCALE = 1.0 / (QK_HEAD_DIM ** 0.5)\nPAGE_SIZE = 1\nNUM_KV_SPLITS = 32\n\n\ndef quantize_fp8(tensor: torch.Tensor) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:\n    finfo = torch.finfo(FP8_DTYPE)\n    amax = tensor.abs().amax().clamp(min=1e-12)\n    scale = amax / finfo.max\n    fp8_tensor = (tensor / scale).clamp(min=finfo.min, max=finfo.max).to(FP8_DTYPE)\n    return fp8_tensor, scale.to(torch.float32).reshape(1)\n\n\ndef _build_metadata(\n    batch_size: int,\n    max_q_len: int,\n    nhead: int,\n    nhead_kv: int,\n    q_dtype: torch.dtype,\n    kv_dtype: torch.dtype,\n    qo_indptr: torch.Tensor,\n    kv_indptr: torch.Tensor,\n    kv_last_page_len: torch.Tensor,\n):\n    info = get_mla_metadata_info_v1(\n        batch_size, max_q_len, nhead, q_dtype, kv_dtype,\n        is_sparse=False, fast_mode=False,\n        num_kv_splits=NUM_KV_SPLITS, intra_batch_mode=True,\n    )\n    work = [torch.empty(s, dtype=t, device=\"cuda\") for s, t in info]\n    (work_metadata, work_indptr, work_info_set,\n     reduce_indptr, reduce_final_map, reduce_partial_map) = work\n\n    get_mla_metadata_v1(\n        qo_indptr, kv_indptr, kv_last_page_len,\n        nhead // nhead_kv,\n        nhead_kv,\n        True,  # is_causal\n        work_metadata, work_info_set, work_indptr,\n        reduce_indptr, reduce_final_map, reduce_partial_map,\n        page_size=PAGE_SIZE,\n        kv_granularity=max(PAGE_SIZE, 16),\n        max_seqlen_qo=max_q_len,\n        uni_seqlen_qo=max_q_len,\n        fast_mode=False,\n        max_split_per_batch=NUM_KV_SPLITS,\n        intra_batch_mode=True,\n        dtype_q=q_dtype,\n        dtype_kv=kv_dtype,\n    )\n\n    return {\n        \"work_meta_data\": work_metadata,\n        \"work_indptr\": work_indptr,\n        \"work_info_set\": work_info_set,\n        \"reduce_indptr\": reduce_indptr,\n        \"reduce_final_map\": reduce_final_map,\n        \"reduce_partial_map\": reduce_partial_map,\n    }\n\n\ndef custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:\n    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config = data\n\n    batch_size = config[\"batch_size\"]\n    nq = config[\"num_heads\"]\n    nkv = config[\"num_kv_heads\"]\n    dq = config[\"qk_head_dim\"]\n    dv = config[\"v_head_dim\"]\n    q_seq_len = config[\"q_seq_len\"]\n\n    q_fp8, q_scale = quantize_fp8(q)\n    kv_buffer_fp8, kv_scale_fp8 = kv_data[\"fp8\"]\n\n    total_kv_len = int(kv_indptr[-1].item())\n    kv_indices = torch.arange(total_kv_len, dtype=torch.int32, device=\"cuda\")\n\n    kv_4d = kv_buffer_fp8.view(\n        kv_buffer_fp8.shape[0], PAGE_SIZE, nkv, kv_buffer_fp8.shape[-1]\n    )\n\n    max_q_len = q_seq_len\n    kv_last_page_len = (kv_indptr[1:] - kv_indptr[:-1]).to(torch.int32)\n\n    meta = _build_metadata(\n        batch_size, max_q_len, nq, nkv,\n        q_fp8.dtype, kv_buffer_fp8.dtype,\n        qo_indptr, kv_indptr, kv_last_page_len,\n    )\n\n    o = torch.empty((q.shape[0], nq, dv), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device=\"cuda\")\n\n    mla_decode_fwd(\n        q_fp8.view(-1, nq, dq),\n        kv_4d,\n        o,\n        qo_indptr,\n        kv_indptr,\n        kv_indices,\n        kv_last_page_len,\n        max_q_len,\n        page_size=PAGE_SIZE,\n        nhead_kv=nkv,\n        sm_scale=SM_SCALE,\n        logit_cap=0.0,\n        num_kv_splits=NUM_KV_SPLITS,\n        q_scale=q_scale,\n        kv_scale=kv_scale_fp8,\n        intra_batch_mode=True,\n        **meta,\n    )\n\n    return o\n"
}
```
