Now I have a comprehensive understanding of the VLIW challenge and the optimization approaches used. Let me synthesize the transferable lessons.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
The VLIW challenge and the Luma AMD Speedrun share a **deep structural parallel**: both involve optimizing low-level kernel execution under severe hardware constraints, with a simulator/evaluator that you can't modify — only your kernel code changes. The key difference is the VLIW had a **local simulator** for rapid iteration (instant feedback), while the AMD challenge requires remote-only submission (~2 min/test, ~10 min/benchmark including JIT).
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

---

## Cross-Pollination Analysis: VLIW → AMD Speedrun

Here's what I extracted from the Anthropic VLIW take-home that's applicable to the Luma AMD kernel work:

### 1. **The "Understand the Machine Before Tuning Parameters" Pattern**

In the VLIW challenge, the winning approach was NOT parameter tweaking — it was **deep architectural understanding**:

- The `VLIWPacker` was the instruction scheduler. Understanding its data-dependency tracking (`last_write`, `last_read`, `last_mem_write`) was key to knowing *why* certain instruction orderings produced different cycle counts.
- The breakthrough was discovering **"Temporal Instruction Leakage"** — the packer greedily scheduling next-round instructions before a barrier was resolved. The fix was injecting explicit data dependencies into `pause` instructions.
- The `multiply_add` fusion in `add_hash_hybrid` (replacing `+` then `<<` with a single `multiply_add`) was a micro-optimization that only made sense once the macro-level scheduling was correct.

**Transfer to AMD Speedrun:** We've been trying parameter tuning (`block_size_M`, `dispatch_policy`, `doweight_stage1`) on a black-box kernel. The VLIW approach says: **read the aiter source code more deeply**. Specifically, the CK (Composable Kernel) 2-stage pipeline selection in `get_2stage_cfgs()` and the tile configuration CSV files that drive auto-tuning. Understanding *why* the auto-tuner picks specific configs for specific shapes could reveal a missed optimization path.

### 2. **The "Two Builders" Architecture**

The VLIW solution had two kernel builders:
- `SimpleKernelBuilder` — correct, straightforward, 32-batch interleaving
- `OptimizedKernelBuilder` — aggressive, with `multiply_add` fusion, crown caching, hash optimization, sharded mux

The simple builder was the **correctness anchor** (like our `ref_kernel` delegation). The optimized builder was the performance explorer. They shared the same `VLIWPacker` infrastructure.

**Transfer to AMD Speedrun:** This is exactly what we're missing. We have `ref_kernel` (the correctness anchor) but no "optimized builder" that works differently from the reference. The VLIW pattern suggests: **build a custom implementation that computes the same result differently**, not just tweak parameters of the reference.

### 3. **"Batched Window Interleaving" — Hiding Latency**

The key VLIW optimization was processing **28-32 independent vectors simultaneously** to hide memory latency:

```python
# From optimizer.py — N_VEC=28 windows processed in pipeline
for r in range(rounds):
    for i in range(chunk_size):
        # Each window's gather load is issued...
        # While previous window's ALU hash is computing
```

The `VLIWPacker` automatically scheduled window N's loads to overlap with window N-1's computation, because there were no data dependencies between windows.

**Transfer to AMD Speedrun:** The aiter `fused_moe` already does batch interleaving internally (2-stage pipeline: stage 1 does gate+up GEMM, stage 2 does down GEMM). But the MLA kernel's `num_kv_splits` is essentially the same concept — splitting work into independent chunks to keep GPU CUs busy. The VLIW lesson is: **the optimal split count depends on the compute-to-memory ratio of the workload**, not a fixed number.

### 4. **"The Barrier Mastery" Lesson — Synchronization Costs**

The VLIW `simple_builder.py` has a critical pattern at line 300:
```python
# This pause MUST follow ALL stores of the round
self.packer.add_slot("flow", ("pause",), reads=tuple(all_barrier_regs))
self.packer.barrier()
```

The data dependency on `all_barrier_regs` forces the packer to NOT schedule the pause until all stores complete. Without this, the simulator produces wrong results — not crashes, but **subtle correctness errors from temporal leakage**.

**Transfer to AMD Speedrun:** This is directly analogous to the `doweight_stage1` correctness failure. The aiter 2-stage pipeline has an implicit barrier between stages. Moving weight multiplication from stage 2 to stage 1 (`doweight_stage1=True`) is like moving computation across a barrier — it changes the mathematical result because the SiLU nonlinearity sits between the stages.

### 5. **"Local Simulation + Perfetto Tracing" — The Iteration Speed Advantage**

The VLIW challenge included a full simulator with Chrome Trace Format output (Perfetto visualization). This meant:
- **Instant feedback** — run the kernel, see the cycle count immediately
- **Visual debugging** — see exactly which slots are idle, where bubbles form
- **Rapid iteration** — try 50 variations in an hour

**Transfer to AMD Speedrun:** This is our **biggest disadvantage**. We have ~2 min test / ~10 min benchmark per iteration. The VLIW approach strongly suggests we should **build a local profiling proxy**. While we can't run MI355X kernels locally (gfx1151 isn't compatible), we could:
- Parse aiter's tuned CSV configs to understand what tile sizes the auto-tuner selects for each shape
- Build a simple roofline model to estimate theoretical throughput
- Use that to filter candidates *before* expensive remote submissions

### 6. **"The Swarm Brainstorm" — Multi-Agent Ideation**

The `swarm_brainstorm.py` file shows a three-model roundtable (DeepSeek-R1 as Architect, Qwen3-Coder as Engineer, DeepSeek as Synthesizer) to brainstorm VLIW optimization strategies. The prompt specifically asks about:
- "Reality Distortion" — exploiting simulator edge cases
- "Predicate Speculation" — speculating both branches
- "Manifold Alignment" — finding patterns in supposedly random data

**Transfer to AMD Speedrun:** We haven't tried this approach for the AMD kernels. A focused swarm brainstorm specifically on "what can be done beyond parameter tuning for MXFP4 MoE on MI355X?" could generate novel directions — e.g., custom quantization strategies, alternative dispatch algorithms, or data layout transformations.

---

## Actionable Next Steps (Informed by VLIW Patterns)

| VLIW Pattern | AMD Speedrun Application | Effort | Expected Impact |
|---|---|---|---|
| Deep source reading | Read aiter CK 2-stage kernel configs, understand tile selection | Medium | Could reveal optimal tile overrides for specific shapes |
| Custom builder (not just params) | Write custom dispatch + GEMM pipeline (bypass `fused_moe`) | **High** | Leaders do this — only path to top 10 |
| Local profiling proxy | Parse tuned CSV configs, build roofline model | Low | Filter candidates before remote submission |
| Swarm brainstorm | Run multi-model debate on MoE optimization strategies | Low | Novel directions we haven't considered |

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**The fundamental lesson from the VLIW work:** Parameter tuning an already-optimized black box has diminishing returns. The VLIW challenge was won by understanding the machine at the instruction scheduling level and building a custom kernel generator. Similarly, the AMD leaderboard leaders aren't tuning `fused_moe` parameters — they're writing custom HIP/ASM kernels that bypass the library entirely. The VLIW "Two Builders" pattern (correctness anchor + optimized explorer) is the right architecture for attempting that.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Would you like me to pursue any of these directions? The lowest-hanging fruit is probably **reading the aiter tuned CSV configs** to understand the auto-tuner's decision space, or **running a swarm brainstorm** for novel MoE optimization strategies. The highest-impact (but highest-effort) path would be writing a custom kernel that bypasses `fused_moe`.

{

  "content": "---\nname: competitive-kernel-optimization-ceiling\ndescription: |\n  Strategy for competitive GPU/hardware kernel optimization when parameter tuning is exhausted.\n  Use when: (1) you've tried all accessible API parameters and none improved performance,\n  (2) leaderboard leaders are 2x+ faster than your reference-matching submission,\n  (3) planning next steps after hitting the aiter/fused_moe/mla_decode_fwd parameter ceiling,\n  (4) cross-referencing a past kernel challenge (e.g., VLIW) for applicable patterns.\n  Key insight: Top entries bypass library APIs entirely. The \"Two Builders\" pattern\n  (correctness anchor + custom explorer) is the structured path to custom kernel work.\nauthor: Claude Code\nversion: 1.0.0\ntags: [amd, gpu, kernel, optimization, competition, vliw, aiter, mi355x, luma-speedrun]\n---\n\n# Competitive Kernel Optimization: Beyond Parameter Tuning\n\n## Problem\n\nAfter exhausting all tunable parameters in a library (e.g., aiter's `fused_moe`), ranking\nstays in the lower half of the leaderboard. The reference implementation is already\nwell-optimized; parameter sweeps either have no effect, cause correctness failures, or\ndegrade performance.\n\n## Context: When to Use This\n\n- All accessible API parameters have been tested (documented in `aiter-kernel-parameter-semantics`)\n- Still ranked outside top 20 with >2x gap to leader\n- Considering whether to abandon vs. continue the challenge\n- Wanting to plan next iteration with higher expected value\n\n## The Decision Hierarchy\n\n### Level 1: Local Proxy (Low effort, immediate)\n\nBefore expensive remote submissions, build a local model of the performance space:\n\n**Parse aiter's tuned CSV configs:**\n```bash\ncat /path/to/aiter/aiter/configs/tuned_fmoe.csv\ncat /path/to/aiter/aiter/configs/model_configs/dsv3_fp4_tuned_fmoe.csv\n```\n\nThese CSVs contain: `(n_experts, batch_size, d_hidden, d_expert, topk, block_m, ...)` → `(kernel_name, cycles)`.\nReading them reveals:\n- Which tile configs the auto-tuner selects for which shapes\n- Whether there are shape-specific configs that differ from the defaults\n- Whether your benchmark shapes have dedicated tuned entries or fall back to defaults\n\n**Roofline model estimate:**\n```python\n# For MXFP4 GEMM: theoretical peak = GPU_TFLOPS * efficiency\n# Compare against actual timing to determine if compute-bound or memory-bound\nbytes_per_token = d_hidden * sizeof(fp4)  # input\nbytes_weights = d_expert * d_hidden * sizeof(fp4)  # per expert, per token\narithmetic_intensity = 2 * d_hidden * d_expert / bytes_weights  # FLOPs/byte\n```\n\n### Level 2: The \"Two Builders\" Pattern (Medium effort)\n\nFrom the Anthropic VLIW take-home challenge (see `research/challenges/anthropic_challenge/`):\n\nThe winning approach used two separate kernel builders:\n- **Builder A (Correctness Anchor):** Delegates to `ref_kernel` or matches reference exactly.\n  Never changes. Used to verify correctness of Builder B.\n- **Builder B (Custom Explorer):** Reimplements the same computation differently.\n  Can be tested against Builder A as ground truth.\n\nFor AMD MoE, this means writing a **custom dispatch + GEMM pipeline** that:\n1. Uses the same shuffled weights and scales (pre-computed by aiter)\n2. Implements its own token sorting (potentially simpler than aiter's)\n3. Calls CK GEMM kernels directly instead of through `fused_moe`\n\n```python\n# Template: Two Builders pattern for AMD kernel submission\nfrom reference import ref_kernel  # Builder A: correctness anchor\n\ndef custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:\n    # Try Builder B; fall back to Builder A on any exception\n    try:\n        return custom_builder(data)\n    except Exception:\n        return ref_kernel(data)  # Safety net during development\n```\n\n### Level 3: Custom HIP/ASM Kernel (High effort, highest impact)\n\nWhat top leaderboard entries do:\n- Write kernel in HIP C++ or assembly, compiled with `hipcc`\n- Bypass the aiter library entirely for the hot path\n- Use hardware-specific optimizations (e.g., MI355X matrix core layouts, gfx950 ISA)\n- Typically requires familiarity with ROCm programming model\n\nThis is only worth pursuing if:\n- You have HIP/CUDA experience\n- The competition has days remaining (not hours)\n- The performance gap justifies the implementation risk\n\n## VLIW Cross-Pollination: Applicable Lessons\n\nThe Anthropic VLIW take-home (`research/challenges/anthropic_challenge/`) shares structural\nparallels with AMD GPU kernel challenges:\n\n| VLIW Concept | AMD Kernel Analog |\n|---|---|\n| Instruction slots (alu, valu, load) | GPU execution units (VALU, SALU, memory) |\n| `VLIWPacker` data-dependency tracking | aiter's 2-stage pipeline dependency between gate+up GEMM and down GEMM |\n| \"Temporal Instruction Leakage\" across barriers | `doweight_stage1=True` leaking weight mul across the SiLU activation barrier |\n| N_VEC=28 window interleaving for latency hiding | `num_kv_splits` in MLA for hiding KV cache memory latency |\n| `multiply_add` fusion optimization | CK `moe_ck2stages_gemm1_*MulABScaleShuffled*` kernel name encodes similar fusion |\n| Local simulator (instant feedback) | Popcorn CLI remote (~2-10 min/iteration) |\n\n**Key VLIW insight transferable to AMD:**\nThe VLIW challenge was NOT won by parameter tuning the packer. It was won by:\n1. Understanding how the scheduler works internally (`last_write`, `last_read` tracking)\n2. Discovering that barriers needed explicit data-dependency injection\n3. Building a custom kernel generator that produced different instruction schedules\n\nEquivalent for AMD: understand how aiter selects kernels (`get_2stage_cfgs()` in `fused_moe.py`),\nwhat the tile configs mean, and build something that uses them differently.\n\n## Iteration Budget Management\n\nWith ~2 min test + ~8-10 min benchmark per remote submission (Luma AMD Speedrun):\n\n| Action | Submits needed | Expected outcome |\n|---|---|---|\n| Parse CSV configs locally | 0 | Shape-specific tile insights |\n| Roofline analysis | 0 | Compute vs memory bound diagnosis |\n| Swarm brainstorm | 0 | Novel directions inventory |\n| Single parameter test | 1-2 | Usually negative (already exhausted) |\n| Custom dispatch prototype | 3-5 | Medium risk, medium reward |\n| Custom HIP kernel | 10+ | High risk, high reward |\n\n**Rule:** If a parameter test requires >2 submits to verify, it's probably not worth it.\nInvest those submits in Builder B prototypes instead.\n\n## Swarm Brainstorm Template\n\nWhen stuck, use `swarm_brainstorm.py` (see `research/challenges/anthropic_challenge/`) as\ntemplate for a multi-model debate. Useful prompts for AMD MoE:\n\n- \"What CK kernel configurations exist for MoE that aren't exposed through fused_moe's API?\"\n- \"Can we reuse the pre-shuffled weight layout with a different dispatch algorithm?\"\n- \"What does the token distribution look like across experts for bs=16 with 256 experts?\n   Does the sparse pattern suggest a different algorithm?\"\n\n## Context: Luma AMD Speedrun Competition\n\n- **Challenge:** `amd-moe-mxfp4`, `amd-mixed-mla`, `amd-mxfp4-mm`\n- **Hardware:** AMD Instinct MI355X (gfx950, ROCm 7.1, Torch 2.10+rocm7.1)\n- **Parameter space exhausted as of March 2026:** All aiter API knobs tested and documented\n  in `aiter-kernel-parameter-semantics` skill\n- **VLIW reference:** `research/challenges/anthropic_challenge/simple_builder.py` (winner),\n  `optimizer.py` (advanced variant with multiply_add fusion and crown caching)\n"

     1→# Luma AMD Speedrun: Optimize Kernels — Research-Informed Plan
     2→
     3→## Context
     4→
     5→Optimize 3 GPU kernels for AMD MI355X leaderboard rankings. Currently scoring 0 points (need top 10 in at least one). All testing is remote-only via Popcorn CLI (~2 min/submit).
     6→
     7→| Kernel | Rank | Our Time | Leader | Gap | Leaderboard |
     8→|--------|------|----------|--------|-----|-------------|
     9→| MoE | 34/43 | 185 us | 145 us | 1.28x | `amd-moe-mxfp4` |
    10→| GEMM | 67/68 | 24 us | 9.7 us | 2.49x | `amd-mxfp4-mm` |
    11→| MLA | 40/54 | 191 us | 4.3 us | 44x | `amd-mixed-mla` |
    12→
    13→**Priority:** GEMM fix (easiest win) → MoE tuning (closest to top 10) → MLA tuning
    14→
    15→## Deep Research Findings (from aiter source code analysis)
    16→
    17→### GEMM: `get_torch_quant` bypasses JIT bug
    18→- `fused_moe.py` imports `get_hip_quant` (not Triton) internally
    19→- `aiter/ops/quant.py` has 3 quant backends: `get_torch_quant` (pure PyTorch), `get_triton_quant` (Triton JIT), `get_hip_quant` (HIP C++)
    20→- Our bug: `get_triton_quant` produces different JIT compilations from different call-sites
    21→- **Fix:** Use `get_torch_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)` which maps to `per_1x32_f4_quant` (deterministic PyTorch) — no JIT dependency
    22→- B is already pre-quantized+shuffled in the input data, so we only need to quantize A
    23→
    24→### MoE: 5 tunable parameters discovered
    25→From `aiter/fused_moe.py` (1828 lines):
    26→1. **`block_size_M`** — tile size along token dim. Default `-1` auto-selects from CSV configs. Override with `32`, `64`, `128`
    27→2. **`moe_sorting_dispatch_policy`** — default `0`. Controls token-to-expert dispatch strategy
    28→3. **`doweight_stage1`** — applies topk_weights in stage 1 vs stage 2
    29→4. **1-stage vs 2-stage** — auto-selected by `get_2stage_cfgs()`. For MXFP4 `q_dtype_a=fp4x2`: 1-stage when `token > 32 && inter_dim % 256 == 0`
    30→5. **OPUS sorting** — env var `AITER_USE_OPUS_MOE_SORTING=1` switches to `moe_sorting_opus_fwd`
    31→6. **`fused_moe_dp_share_expert`** — AMD's own shared expert module in `aiter/fused_moe_dp_shared_expert.py`. Runs shared expert as separate dense op
    32→
    33→### MLA: auto-tuner uses much lower NUM_KV_SPLITS
    34→From `aiter/mla.py` `get_meta_param()`:
    35→- Auto-tuner picks `num_kv_splits` from 1-16 (not 32!) based on: `score = gpu_util * compute_efficiency`
    36→- For FP8, splits capped by `min_block_n` lookup: `{16:128, 32:128, 64:64, 128:32, 256:32, 512:32}` keyed by `nhead * max_seqlen_q`
    37→- `fast_mode=True` in metadata builder allocates `(bs + cu_num) * tiles` work items vs `tile_cnt * cu_num` — much smaller buffers
    38→- Non-persistent mode auto-selects `num_kv_splits` and uses `MAYBE_FINAL_OUT=True` for direct output (skipping reduction when `num_kv_splits==1`)
    39→
    40→## Files to Modify
    41→
    42→| File | Kernel |
    43→|------|--------|
    44→| `.worktrees/spec-luma-amd-speedrun/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py` | GEMM |
    45→| `.worktrees/spec-luma-amd-speedrun/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission.py` | MoE |
    46→| `.worktrees/spec-luma-amd-speedrun/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/kernels/mixed-mla/submission.py` | MLA |
    47→
    48→## Progress Tracking
    49→
    50→- [ ] Task 1: Fix GEMM — use `get_torch_quant` to bypass JIT bug
    51→- [ ] Task 2: Optimize GEMM — shape-dependent routing, try `gemm_afp4wfp4`
    52→- [ ] Task 3: Optimize MoE — sweep `block_size_M`, `doweight_stage1`, dispatch policy
    53→- [ ] Task 4: Optimize MLA — sweep `NUM_KV_SPLITS`, try non-persistent mode, `fast_mode`
    54→
    55→**Total Tasks:** 4 | **Completed:** 0 | **Remaining:** 4
    56→
    57→## Implementation Tasks
    58→
    59→### Task 1: Fix GEMM — `get_torch_quant` bypass (target: < 20 us)
    60→
    61→**Objective:** Replace `ref_kernel` delegation with direct `gemm_a4w4` call using `get_torch_quant` for A quantization.
    62→
    63→**Files:** `kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py`
    64→
    65→**Implementation:**
    66→```python
    67→import torch
    68→import aiter
    69→from aiter import QuantType, dtypes
    70→from aiter.ops.shuffle import shuffle_weight
    71→from task import input_t, output_t
    72→
    73→def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    74→    A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data
    75→    A = A.contiguous()
    76→    # Use get_torch_quant (pure PyTorch, no Triton JIT) to avoid call-site bug
    77→    quant_func = aiter.get_torch_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
    78→    A_q, A_scale = quant_func(A, shuffle=True)
    79→    out = aiter.gemm_a4w4(A_q, B_shuffle, A_scale, B_scale_sh, dtype=dtypes.bf16, bpreshuffle=True)
    80→    return out
    81→```
    82→
    83→**Verify:** `popcorn submit --mode test` passes → `--mode benchmark` < 20 us → `--mode leaderboard`
    84→
    85→**Fallback if `get_torch_quant` shuffle kwarg not supported:** Try `quant_func(A)` without shuffle, then `shuffle_weight(A_q, layout=(16,16))` + `fp4_utils.e8m0_shuffle(A_scale)` manually.
    86→
    87→**Fallback 2:** Try `aiter.get_hip_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)` — the HIP backend used internally by `fused_moe`.
    88→
    89→---
    90→

    93→**Depends on:** Task 1
    94→
    95→**Files:** `kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py`
    96→
    97→**Try in order:**
    98→1. **Triton `gemm_afp4wfp4`** for M >= 64 — `aiter.ops.triton.gemm.basic.gemm_afp4wfp4` has split-K support and auto-tuning configs. May outperform CK `gemm_a4w4` for large M
    99→2. **Shape routing:** M <= 32 → `gemm_a4w4`, M >= 64 → `gemm_afp4wfp4`
   100→3. **`splitk` parameter** for `gemm_a4w4` — default heuristic computes from CU count and tile sizes

     1→from utils import make_match_reference
     2→from task import input_t, output_t
     3→import torch
     4→import torch.nn.functional as F
     5→from typing import Dict, Tuple, Optional
     6→import math
     7→
     8→import aiter
     9→from aiter import ActivationType, QuantType, dtypes
    10→from aiter.fused_moe import fused_moe
    11→from aiter.utility import fp4_utils
    12→from aiter.ops.shuffle import shuffle_weight
    13→
    14→
    15→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    16→# Constants
    17→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    18→MXFP4_BLOCK_SIZE = 32
    19→PAD_ALIGN = 256
    20→
    21→
    22→def _pad_to(x: int, align: int) -> int:
    23→    return (x + align - 1) // align * align
    24→
    25→
    26→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    27→# generate_input: produce all tensors needed by ref_kernel
    28→#
    29→# Models DeepSeek-R1 MoE layer shapes:
    30→#   - d_hidden = 7168
    31→#   - d_expert = moe_intermediate_size (full=2048, or TP-split)
    32→#   - E_total = n_routed_experts + n_shared_experts (257 or 33)
    33→#   - top_k_total = nexpertspertoken + nsharedexperts (8+1=9)
    34→#
    35→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    36→def generate_input(
    37→    dhidden: int,
    38→    dexpert: int,
    39→    nroutedexperts: int,
    40→    nexpertspertoken: int,
    41→    nsharedexperts: int,
    42→    bs: int,
    43→    seed: int,
    44→) -> input_t:
    45→    d_hidden = dhidden
    46→    d_expert = dexpert
    47→    n_routed_experts = nroutedexperts
    48→    n_shared_experts = nsharedexperts
    49→    routed_top_k = nexpertspertoken
    50→    total_top_k = routed_top_k + n_shared_experts   # e.g. 8 + 1 = 9
    51→    E_total = n_routed_experts + n_shared_experts    # e.g. 256 + 1 = 257
    52→    M = bs  # number of tokens
    53→
    54→    # Padded dimensions (AITER MXFP4 requires 256-alignment)
    55→    d_hidden_pad = _pad_to(d_hidden, PAD_ALIGN)
    56→    d_expert_pad = _pad_to(d_expert, PAD_ALIGN)
    57→
    58→    config = {
    59→        "d_hidden": d_hidden,
    60→        "d_expert": d_expert,
    61→        "d_hidden_pad": d_hidden_pad,
    62→        "d_expert_pad": d_expert_pad,
    63→        "n_routed_experts": n_routed_experts,
    64→        "n_shared_experts": n_shared_experts,
    65→        "n_experts_per_token": routed_top_k,
    66→        "total_top_k": total_top_k,
    67→        "bs": M,
    68→    }
    69→
    70→    gen = torch.Generator(device='cuda')
    71→    gen.manual_seed(seed)
    72→
    73→    # ── hidden_states [M, d_hidden] ──
    74→    hidden_states = torch.randn(
    75→        (M, d_hidden), device='cuda', dtype=torch.bfloat16, generator=gen,
    76→    )
    77→
    78→    # ── Router: softmax top-k (routed experts only) ──
    79→    router_weight = torch.randn(
    80→        (n_routed_experts, d_hidden), device='cuda', dtype=torch.bfloat16, generator=gen,
    81→    ) / math.sqrt(d_hidden)
    82→    router_logits = F.linear(hidden_states, router_weight)  # [M, n_routed_experts]
    83→    scores = router_logits.softmax(dim=-1)
    84→    routed_weights, routed_ids = torch.topk(
    85→        scores, k=routed_top_k, dim=-1, sorted=False
    86→    )
    87→    routed_weights = routed_weights.to(torch.float32)
    88→    routed_ids = routed_ids.to(torch.int32)
    89→
    90→    # ── Append shared expert(s): always selected, weight = 1.0 ──
    91→    # Shared experts are indexed as n_routed_experts, n_routed_experts+1, ...
    92→    shared_ids = torch.arange(
    93→        n_routed_experts, E_total, device='cuda', dtype=torch.int32
    94→    ).unsqueeze(0).expand(M, -1)                               # [M, n_shared_experts]
    95→    shared_weights = torch.ones(
    96→        (M, n_shared_experts), device='cuda', dtype=torch.float32
    97→    )
    98→
    99→    topk_ids = torch.cat([routed_ids, shared_ids], dim=-1)        # [M, total_top_k]
   100→    topk_weights = torch.cat([routed_weights, shared_weights], dim=-1)  # [M, total_top_k]
   101→
   102→    gate_up_bf16 = torch.randn(
   103→        (E_total, 2 * d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad), device='cuda', dtype=torch.bfloat16, generator=gen,
   104→    ) / math.sqrt(d_hidden)
   105→    down_bf16 = torch.randn(
   106→        (E_total, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad), device='cuda', dtype=torch.bfloat16, generator=gen,
   107→    ) / math.sqrt(d_expert)
   108→
   109→    torch_quant = aiter.get_torch_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
   110→    gate_up_weight, gate_up_weight_scale = torch_quant(gate_up_bf16, quant_dtype=dtypes.fp4x2)
   111→    down_weight, down_weight_scale = torch_quant(down_bf16, quant_dtype=dtypes.fp4x2)
   112→    gate_up_weight = gate_up_weight.view(E_total, 2 * d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad // 2)
   113→    down_weight = down_weight.view(E_total, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad // 2)
   114→
   115→    gate_up_weight_shuffled = shuffle_weight(gate_up_weight, layout=(16, 16))
   116→    down_weight_shuffled = shuffle_weight(down_weight, layout=(16, 16))
   117→    gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled = fp4_utils.e8m0_shuffle(gate_up_weight_scale)
   118→    down_weight_scale_shuffled = fp4_utils.e8m0_shuffle(down_weight_scale)
   119→
   120→    return (
   121→        hidden_states,                  # [M, d_hidden]                              bf16
   122→        gate_up_weight,                 # [E_total, 2*d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad//2] fp4x2  (raw)
   123→        down_weight,                    # [E_total, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad//2]   fp4x2  (raw)
   124→        gate_up_weight_scale,           # [E_total, 2*d_expert_pad, scale_K]         e8m0   (raw)
   125→        down_weight_scale,              # [E_total, d_hidden_pad, scale_K]           e8m0   (raw)
   126→        gate_up_weight_shuffled,        # [E_total, 2*d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad//2] fp4x2  (pre-shuffled)
   127→        down_weight_shuffled,           # [E_total, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad//2]   fp4x2  (pre-shuffled)
   128→        gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,  # [padded, flat]                             e8m0   (pre-shuffled)
   129→        down_weight_scale_shuffled,     # [padded, flat]                             e8m0   (pre-shuffled)
   130→        topk_weights,                   # [M, total_top_k]                           float32
   131→        topk_ids,                       # [M, total_top_k]                           int32
   132→        config,
   133→    )
   134→
   135→
   136→
   137→
   138→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   139→# ref_kernel_pytorch: pure PyTorch implementation (dequant + matmul)
   140→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   141→def _dequant_mxfp4(weight_fp4, scale_e8m0):
   142→    """
   143→    Dequantize MXFP4 weight to float32.
   144→
   145→    weight_fp4:  [N, K//2]  fp4x2  (raw, not shuffled)
   146→    scale_e8m0:  [padded_N, ceil(K/32)] e8m0   (M-dim padded to 256-align by dynamic_mxfp4_quant)
   147→
   148→    Returns: [N, K] float32
   149→    """
   150→    # fp4x2 -> float32 lookup: [N, K]
   151→    w_f32 = fp4_utils.mxfp4_to_f32(weight_fp4)            # [N, K]
   152→    # e8m0 -> float32 power-of-2 scale: [padded_N, scale_K]
   153→    s_f32 = fp4_utils.e8m0_to_f32(scale_e8m0)             # [padded_N, scale_K]
   154→    N, K = w_f32.shape
   155→    # Trim scale rows to match weight rows (scale M-dim is padded to 256)
   156→    s_f32 = s_f32[:N, :]
   157→    # Broadcast scale across block_size=32 columns
   158→    s_f32 = s_f32.repeat_interleave(MXFP4_BLOCK_SIZE, dim=-1)[:, :K]  # [N, K]
   159→    return w_f32 * s_f32
   160→
   161→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   162→# ref_kernel_pytorch: pure PyTorch implementation (dequant + matmul)
   163→# will not run. only for reference
   164→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   165→def ref_kernel_pytorch(data: input_t) -> output_t:
   166→    """
   167→    Pure PyTorch reference: dequantize MXFP4 weights -> bf16 matmul -> SwiGLU -> matmul.
   168→    Uses the raw (un-shuffled) weights.
   169→    """
   170→    (
   171→        hidden_states,             # [M, d_hidden]          bf16
   172→        gate_up_weight,            # [E, 2*d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad//2]  fp4x2
   173→        down_weight,               # [E, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad//2]    fp4x2
   174→        gate_up_weight_scale,      # [E, 2*d_expert_pad, scale_K]          e8m0
   175→        down_weight_scale,         # [E, d_hidden_pad, scale_K]            e8m0
   176→        gate_up_weight_shuffled,
   177→        down_weight_shuffled,
   178→        gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,
   179→        down_weight_scale_shuffled,
   180→        topk_weights,              # [M, top_k]  float32
   181→        topk_ids,                  # [M, top_k]  int32
   182→        config,
   183→    ) = data
   184→
   185→    d_hidden = config["d_hidden"]
   186→    d_expert = config["d_expert"]
   187→    d_hidden_pad = config["d_hidden_pad"]
   188→    d_expert_pad = config["d_expert_pad"]
   189→    M = hidden_states.shape[0]
   190→    top_k = topk_ids.shape[1]
   191→    E = gate_up_weight.shape[0]
   192→
   193→    # Dequantize all expert weights to float32
   194→    # gate_up: [E, 2*d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad] -> trim to [E, 2*d_expert, d_hidden]
   195→    # down:    [E, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad]    -> trim to [E, d_hidden, d_expert]
   196→    gate_up_dq = torch.stack([
   197→        _dequant_mxfp4(gate_up_weight[e], gate_up_weight_scale[e])
   198→        for e in range(E)
   199→    ])  # [E, 2*d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad]
   200→    gate_up_dq = gate_up_dq[:, :2 * d_expert, :d_hidden].to(torch.bfloat16)
   201→
   202→    down_dq = torch.stack([
   203→        _dequant_mxfp4(down_weight[e], down_weight_scale[e])
   204→        for e in range(E)
   205→    ])  # [E, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad]
   206→    down_dq = down_dq[:, :d_hidden, :d_expert].to(torch.bfloat16)
   207→
   208→    # Split gate_up -> gate [E, d_expert, d_hidden], up [E, d_expert, d_hidden]
   209→    gate_w, up_w = gate_up_dq.chunk(2, dim=1)  # each [E, d_expert, d_hidden]
   210→
   211→    # Per-token MoE forward
   212→    output = torch.zeros((M, d_hidden), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device=hidden_states.device)
   213→
   214→    for i in range(M):
   215→        x = hidden_states[i]  # [d_hidden]
   216→        for k in range(top_k):
   217→            eid = topk_ids[i, k].item()
   218→            w = topk_weights[i, k].item()
   219→
   220→            # Stage 1: gate_proj + up_proj + SwiGLU
   221→            gate_out = F.silu(x @ gate_w[eid].T)     # [d_expert]
   222→            up_out = x @ up_w[eid].T                  # [d_expert]
   223→            intermediate = gate_out * up_out           # [d_expert]
   224→
   225→            # Stage 2: down_proj
   226→            # down_dq[eid] is [d_hidden, d_expert], .T is [d_expert, d_hidden]
   227→            expert_out = intermediate @ down_dq[eid].T  # [d_hidden]
   228→
   229→            output[i] += w * expert_out
   230→
   231→    return output
   232→
   233→
   234→
   235→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   236→# ref_kernel: calls AITER fused_moe with MXFP4 quantized weights
   237→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   238→def ref_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
   239→    """
   240→    Reference implementation using AITER's fused_moe kernel with MXFP4 quantized weights.
   241→
   242→    Input data tuple (E = n_routed_experts + n_shared_experts, total_top_k = routed + shared):
   243→        hidden_states:                [M, d_hidden]                           bf16
   244→        gate_up_weight:               [E, 2*d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad//2]    fp4x2  (raw, before shuffle)
   245→        down_weight:                  [E, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad//2]      fp4x2  (raw, before shuffle)
   246→        gate_up_weight_scale:         [E, 2*d_expert_pad, scale_K]            e8m0   (raw, before shuffle)
   247→        down_weight_scale:            [E, d_hidden_pad, scale_K]              e8m0   (raw, before shuffle)
   248→        gate_up_weight_shuffled:      [E, 2*d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad//2]    fp4x2  (pre-shuffled)
   249→        down_weight_shuffled:         [E, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad//2]      fp4x2  (pre-shuffled)
   250→        gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled:[padded, flat]                          e8m0   (pre-shuffled)
   251→        down_weight_scale_shuffled:   [padded, flat]                          e8m0   (pre-shuffled)
   252→        topk_weights:                 [M, total_top_k]                        float32
   253→        topk_ids:                     [M, total_top_k]                        int32
   254→        config:                       dict
   255→
   256→    Returns:
   257→        output: [M, d_hidden] bf16
   258→    """
   259→    (
   260→        hidden_states,
   261→        gate_up_weight,
   262→        down_weight,
   263→        gate_up_weight_scale,
   264→        down_weight_scale,
   265→        gate_up_weight_shuffled,
   266→        down_weight_shuffled,
   267→        gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,
   268→        down_weight_scale_shuffled,
   269→        topk_weights,
   270→        topk_ids,
   271→        config,
   272→    ) = data
   273→
   274→    hidden_pad = config["d_hidden_pad"] - config["d_hidden"]
   275→    intermediate_pad = config["d_expert_pad"] - config["d_expert"]
   276→
   277→    output = fused_moe(
   278→        hidden_states,
   279→        gate_up_weight_shuffled,
   280→        down_weight_shuffled,
   281→        topk_weights,
   282→        topk_ids,
   283→        expert_mask=None,
   284→        activation=ActivationType.Silu,
   285→        quant_type=QuantType.per_1x32,  # MXFP4 uses per_1x32 block scaling
   286→        doweight_stage1=False,
   287→        w1_scale=gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,
   288→        w2_scale=down_weight_scale_shuffled,
   289→        a1_scale=None,
   290→        a2_scale=None,
   291→        hidden_pad=hidden_pad,
   292→        intermediate_pad=intermediate_pad,
   293→    )
   294→
   295→    return output
   296→
   297→
   298→
   299→check_implementation = make_match_reference(ref_kernel, rtol=5e-2, atol=5e-2)
   300→
```

     1→"""
     2→FP4 quant + FP4 GEMM reference: bf16 A, MXFP4 B -> MXFP4 per-1x32 quant A -> gemm_a4w4 -> bf16 C.
     3→Quant logic follows aiter op_tests/test_gemm_a4w4.py (get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)).
     4→"""
     5→import torch
     6→from task import input_t, output_t
     7→from utils import make_match_reference
     8→from aiter import QuantType,dtypes
     9→import aiter
    10→from aiter.ops.shuffle import shuffle_weight
    11→# K must be divisible by 64 (scale group 32 and fp4 pack 2)
    12→SCALE_GROUP_SIZE = 32
    13→
    14→def generate_input(m: int, n: int, k: int, seed: int):# -> input_t:
    15→    """
    16→    Generate random bf16 inputs A [m, k], B [n, k] and quantized MXFP4 B, shuffled B and B_scale.
    17→
    18→    Returns:
    19→        Tuple of (A, B), both bf16 on cuda.
    20→    """
    21→    assert k % 64 == 0, "k must be divisible by 64 (scale group 32 and fp4 pack 2)"
    22→    gen = torch.Generator(device="cuda")
    23→    gen.manual_seed(seed)
    24→    A = torch.randn((m, k), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda", generator=gen)
    25→    B = torch.randn((n, k), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda", generator=gen)
    26→    
    27→    # quantized mxfp4 B
    28→    quant_func = aiter.get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
    29→    B_q, B_scale_sh = quant_func(B, shuffle=True)
    30→    
    31→    # shuffle B(weight) to (16,16) tile coalesced
    32→    B_shuffle = shuffle_weight(B_q, layout=(16, 16))
    33→    return (A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh)
    34→
    35→def run_torch_fp4_mm(
    36→    x: torch.Tensor,
    37→    w: torch.Tensor,
    38→    x_scales: torch.Tensor,
    39→    w_scales: torch.Tensor,
    40→    dtype: torch.dtype = torch.bfloat16,
    41→) -> torch.Tensor:
    42→    """
    43→    PyTorch reference: dequant MXFP4 + E8M0 scale -> f32 -> mm -> dtype.
    44→    Same logic as aiter op_tests/test_gemm_a4w4.run_torch.
    45→    x: [m, k//2] fp4 packed, w: [n, k//2] fp4 packed
    46→    x_scales: [m, k//32] E8M0, w_scales: [n, k//32] E8M0
    47→    Returns: [m, n] in dtype
    48→    """
    49→    from aiter.utility import fp4_utils
    50→
    51→    m, _ = x.shape
    52→    n, _ = w.shape
    53→    # fp4 packed -> f32
    54→    x_f32 = fp4_utils.mxfp4_to_f32(x)
    55→    w_f32 = fp4_utils.mxfp4_to_f32(w)
    56→    # E8M0 scale: [*, k//32] -> repeat 32 along k -> f32
    57→    x_scales = x_scales[:m].repeat_interleave(SCALE_GROUP_SIZE, dim=1)
    58→    x_scales_f32 = fp4_utils.e8m0_to_f32(x_scales)
    59→    x_f32 = x_f32 * x_scales_f32
    60→    w_scales = w_scales[:n].repeat_interleave(SCALE_GROUP_SIZE, dim=1)
    61→    w_scales_f32 = fp4_utils.e8m0_to_f32(w_scales)
    62→    w_f32 = w_f32 * w_scales_f32
    63→    return torch.mm(x_f32, w_f32.T).to(dtype)[:m, :n]
    64→
    65→
    66→def ref_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    67→    """
    68→    Reference: MXFP4 per-1x32 quant on A and B; both PyTorch ref and gemm_a4w4 are given.
    69→    Returns gemm_a4w4 for check_implementation.
    70→    """
    71→    A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data
    72→    A = A.contiguous()
    73→    B = B.contiguous()
    74→    m, k = A.shape
    75→    n, _ = B.shape
    76→    
    77→    # 1) PyTorch impl just for your reference: dequant fp4 + e8m0 -> f32 -> mm -> bf16
    78→    # Per-1x32 MXFP4 quant
    79→    # quant_func = aiter.get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
    80→    # quant_func(x, shuffle=False) -> (dtypes.fp4x2, scale); scale layout matches gemm_a4w4
    81→    # A_q, A_scale = quant_func(A, shuffle=False)
    82→    # B_q, B_scale = quant_func(B, shuffle=False)
    83→
    84→    # gemm_a4w4 expects A [M,K/2], B [N,K/2] as dtypes.fp4x2; A_scale/B_scale [*,K/32] E8M0
    85→    # quant_func returns scale as dtypes.fp8_e8m0; gemm_a4w4 accepts E8M0, no view to uint8 needed
    86→    # slice to exact shapes [m,k_scale] / [n,k_scale] (quant may return padded scale)
    87→    
    88→    # k_scale = k // SCALE_GROUP_SIZE
    89→    # A_scale = A_scale[:m, :k_scale].contiguous()
    90→    # B_scale = B_scale[:n, :k_scale].contiguous()
    91→    # out_torch = run_torch_fp4_mm(A_q, B_q, A_scale, B_scale, torch.bfloat16)
    92→
    93→    # 2) aiter.gemm_a4w4 path: needs shuffled B_q and shuffled scales (see test_gemm_a4w4.py:102-105)
    94→    # Per-1x32 MXFP4 quant
    95→    quant_func = aiter.get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
    96→    A_q, A_scale_sh = quant_func(A, shuffle=True)
    97→    # to be noted, aiter also has other a4w4 implements using triton, https://github.com/ROCm/aiter/blob/main/aiter/ops/triton/gemm/basic/gemm_afp4wfp4.py
    98→    out_gemm = aiter.gemm_a4w4(
    99→        A_q,
   100→        B_shuffle,
   101→        A_scale_sh,
   102→        B_scale_sh,
   103→        dtype=dtypes.bf16,
   104→        bpreshuffle=True,
   105→    )
   106→    return out_gemm
   107→
   108→check_implementation = make_match_reference(ref_kernel, rtol=1e-02, atol=1e-02)

     2→MXFP4 GEMM submission for AMD MI355X.
     3→
     4→Delegates to ref_kernel to guarantee correctness. The Triton JIT call-site
     5→bug on AMD ROCm prevents reimplementing the quantization path in a separate

     7→for gemm_a4w4 (different data layout between torch_quant and triton_quant).

    10→the aiter gemm_a4w4 kernel performance. Further optimization requires

    14→from task import input_t, output_t
    15→
    16→

     1→"""
     2→MLA decode kernel for AMD MI355X.
     3→
     4→Uses aiter's mla_decode_fwd persistent-mode kernel with fp8 Q + fp8 KV.
     5→Matches reference parameters exactly — fast_mode and adaptive splits
     6→both tested slower than the reference defaults.
     7→"""
     8→import torch
     9→from task import input_t, output_t
    10→from aiter import dtypes as aiter_dtypes
    11→from aiter.mla import mla_decode_fwd
    12→from aiter import get_mla_metadata_info_v1, get_mla_metadata_v1
    13→
    14→FP8_DTYPE = aiter_dtypes.fp8
    15→
    16→# MLA constants (DeepSeek R1 forward_absorb)
    17→NUM_KV_HEADS = 1
    18→KV_LORA_RANK = 512
    19→QK_HEAD_DIM = 576
    20→V_HEAD_DIM = KV_LORA_RANK       # 512
    21→SM_SCALE = 1.0 / (QK_HEAD_DIM ** 0.5)
    22→PAGE_SIZE = 1
    23→NUM_KV_SPLITS = 32
    24→
    25→
    26→def quantize_fp8(tensor: torch.Tensor) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
    27→    finfo = torch.finfo(FP8_DTYPE)
    28→    amax = tensor.abs().amax().clamp(min=1e-12)
    29→    scale = amax / finfo.max
    30→    fp8_tensor = (tensor / scale).clamp(min=finfo.min, max=finfo.max).to(FP8_DTYPE)
    31→    return fp8_tensor, scale.to(torch.float32).reshape(1)
    32→
    33→
    34→def _build_metadata(
    35→    batch_size: int,
    36→    max_q_len: int,
    37→    nhead: int,
    38→    nhead_kv: int,
    39→    q_dtype: torch.dtype,
    40→    kv_dtype: torch.dtype,
    41→    qo_indptr: torch.Tensor,
    42→    kv_indptr: torch.Tensor,
    43→    kv_last_page_len: torch.Tensor,
    44→):
    45→    info = get_mla_metadata_info_v1(
    46→        batch_size, max_q_len, nhead, q_dtype, kv_dtype,
    47→        is_sparse=False, fast_mode=False,
    48→        num_kv_splits=NUM_KV_SPLITS, intra_batch_mode=True,
    49→    )
    50→    work = [torch.empty(s, dtype=t, device="cuda") for s, t in info]
    51→    (work_metadata, work_indptr, work_info_set,
    52→     reduce_indptr, reduce_final_map, reduce_partial_map) = work
    53→
    54→    get_mla_metadata_v1(
    55→        qo_indptr, kv_indptr, kv_last_page_len,
    56→        nhead // nhead_kv,
    57→        nhead_kv,
    58→        True,  # is_causal
    59→        work_metadata, work_info_set, work_indptr,
    60→        reduce_indptr, reduce_final_map, reduce_partial_map,
    61→        page_size=PAGE_SIZE,
    62→        kv_granularity=max(PAGE_SIZE, 16),
    63→        max_seqlen_qo=max_q_len,
    64→        uni_seqlen_qo=max_q_len,
    65→        fast_mode=False,
    66→        max_split_per_batch=NUM_KV_SPLITS,
    67→        intra_batch_mode=True,
    68→        dtype_q=q_dtype,
    69→        dtype_kv=kv_dtype,
    70→    )
    71→
    72→    return {
    73→        "work_meta_data": work_metadata,
    74→        "work_indptr": work_indptr,
    75→        "work_info_set": work_info_set,
    76→        "reduce_indptr": reduce_indptr,
    77→        "reduce_final_map": reduce_final_map,
    78→        "reduce_partial_map": reduce_partial_map,
    79→    }
    80→
    81→
    82→def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    83→    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config = data
    84→
    85→    batch_size = config["batch_size"]
    86→    nq = config["num_heads"]
    87→    nkv = config["num_kv_heads"]
    88→    dq = config["qk_head_dim"]
    89→    dv = config["v_head_dim"]
    90→    q_seq_len = config["q_seq_len"]
    91→
    92→    q_fp8, q_scale = quantize_fp8(q)
    93→    kv_buffer_fp8, kv_scale_fp8 = kv_data["fp8"]
    94→
    95→    total_kv_len = int(kv_indptr[-1].item())
    96→    kv_indices = torch.arange(total_kv_len, dtype=torch.int32, device="cuda")
    97→
    98→    kv_4d = kv_buffer_fp8.view(
    99→        kv_buffer_fp8.shape[0], PAGE_SIZE, nkv, kv_buffer_fp8.shape[-1]
   100→    )
   101→
   102→    max_q_len = q_seq_len
   103→    kv_last_page_len = (kv_indptr[1:] - kv_indptr[:-1]).to(torch.int32)
   104→
   105→    meta = _build_metadata(
   106→        batch_size, max_q_len, nq, nkv,
   107→        q_fp8.dtype, kv_buffer_fp8.dtype,
   108→        qo_indptr, kv_indptr, kv_last_page_len,
   109→    )
   110→
   111→    o = torch.empty((q.shape[0], nq, dv), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda")
   112→
   113→    mla_decode_fwd(
   114→        q_fp8.view(-1, nq, dq),
   115→        kv_4d,
   116→        o,
   117→        qo_indptr,
   118→        kv_indptr,
   119→        kv_indices,
   120→        kv_last_page_len,
   121→        max_q_len,
   122→        page_size=PAGE_SIZE,
   123→        nhead_kv=nkv,
   124→        sm_scale=SM_SCALE,
   125→        logit_cap=0.0,
   126→        num_kv_splits=NUM_KV_SPLITS,
   127→        q_scale=q_scale,
   128→        kv_scale=kv_scale_fp8,
   129→        intra_batch_mode=True,
   130→        **meta,
   131→    )
   132→
   133→    return o
   134→

     1→"""
     2→Reference implementation for MLA (Multi-head Latent Attention) decode kernel.
     3→
     4→Uses aiter MLA kernels (mla_decode_fwd) as the reference.
     5→DeepSeek R1 forward_absorb MLA: absorbed q (576), compressed kv_buffer (576),
     6→output v_head_dim = kv_lora_rank = 512.
     7→
     8→The input provides:
     9→  q:       (total_q, num_heads, 576) bfloat16 — absorbed query (num_heads = 128 // tp)
    10→  kv_data: dict with KV cache in three formats:
    11→    "bf16":  Tensor  (total_kv, 1, 576)  bfloat16          — highest precision
    12→    "fp8":   (Tensor, Tensor)  kv_buffer fp8 + scalar scale — per-tensor quantized
    13→    "mxfp4": (Tensor, Tensor)  kv_buffer fp4x2 + fp8_e8m0  — block-32 quantized
    14→  The reference quantizes Q to fp8 on-the-fly inside ref_kernel.
    15→
    16→The reference kernel quantizes Q to fp8 on-the-fly and uses fp8 KV (a8w8 kernel),
    17→which is ~2-3x faster than bf16 on MI355X with negligible accuracy loss.
    18→
    19→Decode only — persistent mode with get_mla_metadata_v1.
    20→"""
    21→
    22→import torch
    23→import torch.nn.functional as F
    24→from task import input_t, output_t
    25→from utils import make_match_reference
    26→
    27→from aiter.mla import mla_decode_fwd
    28→from aiter import dtypes as aiter_dtypes
    29→from aiter import get_mla_metadata_info_v1, get_mla_metadata_v1
    30→from aiter.utility.fp4_utils import (
    31→    dynamic_mxfp4_quant,
    32→    mxfp4_to_f32,
    33→    e8m0_to_f32,
    34→)
    35→
    36→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    37→# DeepSeek R1 latent MQA constants (forward_absorb path)
    38→# https://huggingface.co/deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-R1-0528/blob/main/config.json
    39→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    40→TOTAL_NUM_HEADS = 128
    41→NUM_KV_HEADS = 1
    42→KV_LORA_RANK = 512
    43→QK_ROPE_HEAD_DIM = 64
    44→QK_HEAD_DIM = KV_LORA_RANK + QK_ROPE_HEAD_DIM   # 576
    45→V_HEAD_DIM = KV_LORA_RANK                        # 512
    46→SM_SCALE = 1.0 / (QK_HEAD_DIM ** 0.5)
    47→
    48→PAGE_SIZE = 1
    49→NUM_KV_SPLITS = 32
    50→
    51→# FP8 dtype (platform-specific via aiter)
    52→FP8_DTYPE = aiter_dtypes.fp8
    53→
    54→# Query dtype for the reference kernel: "fp8" or "bf16"
    55→Q_DTYPE = "fp8"
    56→
    57→# KV cache dtype for the reference kernel: "fp8" or "bf16"
    58→KV_DTYPE = "fp8"
    59→
    60→
    61→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    62→# FP8 quantization (sglang style: dynamic per-tensor)
    63→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    64→
    65→def quantize_fp8(tensor: torch.Tensor) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
    66→    """
    67→    Dynamic per-tensor FP8 quantization (following sglang scaled_fp8_quant).
    68→
    69→    Args:
    70→        tensor: bf16 tensor to quantize
    71→
    72→    Returns:
    73→        (fp8_tensor, scale) where scale is a scalar float32 tensor.
    74→        Dequantize: fp8_tensor.to(bf16) * scale
    75→    """
    76→    finfo = torch.finfo(FP8_DTYPE)
    77→    amax = tensor.abs().amax().clamp(min=1e-12)
    78→    scale = amax / finfo.max
    79→    fp8_tensor = (tensor / scale).clamp(min=finfo.min, max=finfo.max).to(FP8_DTYPE)
    80→    return fp8_tensor, scale.to(torch.float32).reshape(1)
    81→
    82→
    83→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    84→# MXFP4 quantization (aiter native: block-32, fp4x2 + fp8_e8m0 dtypes)
    85→# Uses aiter.utility.fp4_utils.dynamic_mxfp4_quant
    86→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    87→
    88→def quantize_mxfp4(tensor: torch.Tensor) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
    89→    """
    90→    MXFP4 block-wise quantization using aiter's dynamic_mxfp4_quant.
    91→
    92→    Block size = 32. Each block gets an E8M0 scale factor.
    93→    Two FP4 E2M1 values are packed per byte.
    94→
    95→    Args:
    96→        tensor: bf16 tensor of shape [B, M, N] (N must be divisible by 32)
    97→
    98→    Returns:
    99→        (fp4_data, scale_e8m0)
   100→        - fp4_data:   shape [B, M, N//2] in aiter_dtypes.fp4x2
   101→        - scale_e8m0: shape [B*M, ceil(N/32)] padded, in aiter_dtypes.fp8_e8m0
   102→    """
   103→    orig_shape = tensor.shape  # (B, M, N)
   104→    B, M, N = orig_shape
   105→
   106→    # dynamic_mxfp4_quant expects 2D: (B*M, N)
   107→    tensor_2d = tensor.reshape(B * M, N)
   108→    fp4_data_2d, scale_e8m0 = dynamic_mxfp4_quant(tensor_2d)
   109→
   110→    # Reshape fp4_data back to 3D: (B, M, N//2)
   111→    fp4_data = fp4_data_2d.view(B, M, N // 2)
   112→
   113→    return fp4_data, scale_e8m0
   114→
   115→
   116→def dequantize_mxfp4(
   117→    fp4_data: torch.Tensor,
   118→    scale_e8m0: torch.Tensor,
   119→    orig_shape: tuple,
   120→    dtype: torch.dtype = torch.bfloat16,
   121→) -> torch.Tensor:
   122→    """
   123→    Dequantize MXFP4 tensor using aiter utilities.
   124→
   125→    Note: dynamic_mxfp4_quant may pad both row and block dimensions in scale_e8m0.
   126→    We trim scales to match the actual data dimensions.
   127→
   128→    Args:
   129→        fp4_data:   packed FP4 data, shape [B, M, N//2] in fp4x2 or uint8
   130→        scale_e8m0: E8M0 block scale factors (possibly padded) in fp8_e8m0
   131→        orig_shape: original (B, M, N) for reshaping
   132→        dtype:      output dtype
   133→
   134→    Returns:
   135→        Dequantized tensor of shape orig_shape.
   136→    """
   137→    B, M, N = orig_shape
   138→    num_rows = B * M
   139→    block_size = 32
   140→    num_blocks = N // block_size  # actual blocks needed (e.g. 576/32 = 18)
   141→
   142→    # Unpack FP4 to float32: mxfp4_to_f32 expects (..., N//2) -> (..., N)
   143→    fp4_data_2d = fp4_data.reshape(num_rows, N // 2)
   144→    float_vals = mxfp4_to_f32(fp4_data_2d)  # (num_rows, N)
   145→
   146→    # Convert E8M0 scales to float32 and trim padded dimensions
   147→    scale_f32 = e8m0_to_f32(scale_e8m0)  # (padded_rows, padded_blocks)
   148→    scale_f32 = scale_f32[:num_rows, :num_blocks]  # (num_rows, num_blocks)
   149→
   150→    # Apply block scales
   151→    float_vals_blocked = float_vals.view(num_rows, num_blocks, block_size)
   152→    scaled = float_vals_blocked * scale_f32.unsqueeze(-1)
   153→
   154→    return scaled.view(B, M, N).to(dtype)
   155→
   156→
   157→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   158→# Persistent mode metadata helpers
   159→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   160→
   161→def _make_mla_decode_metadata(
   162→    batch_size: int,
   163→    max_q_len: int,
   164→    nhead: int,
   165→    nhead_kv: int,
   166→    q_dtype: torch.dtype,
   167→    kv_dtype: torch.dtype,
   168→    qo_indptr: torch.Tensor,
   169→    kv_indptr: torch.Tensor,
   170→    kv_last_page_len: torch.Tensor,
   171→    num_kv_splits: int = NUM_KV_SPLITS,
   172→):
   173→    """Allocate and populate work buffers for persistent mla_decode_fwd."""
   174→    info = get_mla_metadata_info_v1(
   175→        batch_size, max_q_len, nhead, q_dtype, kv_dtype,
   176→        is_sparse=False, fast_mode=False,
   177→        num_kv_splits=num_kv_splits, intra_batch_mode=True,
   178→    )
   179→    work = [torch.empty(s, dtype=t, device="cuda") for s, t in info]
   180→    (work_metadata, work_indptr, work_info_set,
   181→     reduce_indptr, reduce_final_map, reduce_partial_map) = work
   182→
   183→    # Populate the metadata buffers
   184→    get_mla_metadata_v1(
   185→        qo_indptr, kv_indptr, kv_last_page_len,
   186→        nhead // nhead_kv,   # num_heads_per_head_k
   187→        nhead_kv,            # num_heads_k
   188→        True,                # is_causal
   189→        work_metadata, work_info_set, work_indptr,
   190→        reduce_indptr, reduce_final_map, reduce_partial_map,
   191→        page_size=PAGE_SIZE,
   192→        kv_granularity=max(PAGE_SIZE, 16),
   193→        max_seqlen_qo=max_q_len,
   194→        uni_seqlen_qo=max_q_len,
   195→        fast_mode=False,
   196→        max_split_per_batch=num_kv_splits,
   197→        intra_batch_mode=True,
   198→        dtype_q=q_dtype,
   199→        dtype_kv=kv_dtype,
   200→    )
   201→
   202→    return {
   203→        "work_meta_data": work_metadata,
   204→        "work_indptr": work_indptr,
   205→        "work_info_set": work_info_set,
   206→        "reduce_indptr": reduce_indptr,
   207→        "reduce_final_map": reduce_final_map,
   208→        "reduce_partial_map": reduce_partial_map,
   209→    }
   210→
   211→
   212→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   213→# Aiter reference kernel (decode only)
   214→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   215→
   216→def _aiter_mla_decode(
   217→    q: torch.Tensor,
   218→    kv_buffer: torch.Tensor,
   219→    qo_indptr: torch.Tensor,
   220→    kv_indptr: torch.Tensor,
   221→    config: dict,
   222→    q_scale: torch.Tensor | None = None,
   223→    kv_scale: torch.Tensor | None = None,
   224→) -> torch.Tensor:
   225→    """
   226→    MLA decode attention using aiter persistent-mode kernel.
   227→
   228→    Supports multiple Q/KV dtype combinations:
   229→      - Q_DTYPE="fp8":  fp8 Q + fp8 KV (a8w8) — fastest on MI355X
   230→      - Q_DTYPE="bf16": bf16 Q + bf16 KV (a16w16) — highest precision
   231→
   232→    q:          (total_q, num_heads, 576)  fp8 or bf16
   233→    kv_buffer:  (total_kv, 1, 576)         fp8 or bf16
   234→    q_scale:    scalar float32 (required for fp8 Q, None for bf16)
   235→    kv_scale:   scalar float32 (required for fp8 KV, None for bf16)
   236→    """
   237→    batch_size = config["batch_size"]
   238→    nq = config["num_heads"]
   239→    nkv = config["num_kv_heads"]
   240→    dq = config["qk_head_dim"]
   241→    dv = config["v_head_dim"]
   242→    q_seq_len = config["q_seq_len"]
   243→
   244→    total_kv_len = int(kv_indptr[-1].item())
   245→    kv_indices = torch.arange(total_kv_len, dtype=torch.int32, device="cuda")
   246→
   247→    # Reshape kv_buffer to 4D for aiter: (total_kv, page_size, nhead_kv, dim)
   248→    kv_buffer_4d = kv_buffer.view(kv_buffer.shape[0], PAGE_SIZE, nkv, kv_buffer.shape[-1])
   249→
   250→    max_q_len = q_seq_len
   251→    kv_last_page_len = (kv_indptr[1:] - kv_indptr[:-1]).to(torch.int32)
   252→
   253→    # Build persistent-mode metadata
   254→    meta = _make_mla_decode_metadata(
   255→        batch_size, max_q_len, nq, nkv,
   256→        q.dtype, kv_buffer.dtype,
   257→        qo_indptr, kv_indptr, kv_last_page_len,
   258→        num_kv_splits=NUM_KV_SPLITS,
   259→    )
   260→
   261→    o = torch.empty((q.shape[0], nq, dv), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda")
   262→    mla_decode_fwd(
   263→        q.view(-1, nq, dq),
   264→        kv_buffer_4d,
   265→        o,
   266→        qo_indptr,
   267→        kv_indptr,
   268→        kv_indices,
   269→        kv_last_page_len,
   270→        max_q_len,
   271→        page_size=PAGE_SIZE,
   272→        nhead_kv=nkv,
   273→        sm_scale=SM_SCALE,
   274→        logit_cap=0.0,
   275→        num_kv_splits=NUM_KV_SPLITS,
   276→        q_scale=q_scale,
   277→        kv_scale=kv_scale,
   278→        intra_batch_mode=True,
   279→        **meta,
   280→    )
   281→    return o
   282→
   283→
   284→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   285→# generate_input / ref_kernel / check_implementation
   286→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   287→
   288→def generate_input(batchsize: int, qseqlen: int, kvseqlen: int, tp: int, seed: int) -> input_t:
   289→    """
   290→    Generate absorbed q and compressed kv_buffer for MLA decode.
   291→
   292→    Args:
   293→        tp: tensor parallelism degree (4 or 8). num_heads = TOTAL_NUM_HEADS // tp.
   294→
   295→    Returns all three KV cache formats in kv_data dict:
   296→      kv_data = {
   297→        "bf16":  Tensor               — (total_kv, 1, 576) bfloat16
   298→        "fp8":   (Tensor, Tensor)     — kv_buffer fp8 + scalar scale
   299→        "mxfp4": (Tensor, Tensor)     — kv_buffer fp4x2 + fp8_e8m0 scale
   300→      }
   301→    """
   302→    assert TOTAL_NUM_HEADS % tp == 0, f"TOTAL_NUM_HEADS ({TOTAL_NUM_HEADS}) must be divisible by tp ({tp})"
   303→    num_heads = TOTAL_NUM_HEADS // tp
   304→
   305→    gen = torch.Generator(device="cuda")
   306→    gen.manual_seed(seed)
   307→
   308→    total_q = batchsize * qseqlen
   309→    total_kv = batchsize * kvseqlen
   310→
   311→    # Absorbed query: (total_q, num_heads, 576) bf16
   312→    q = torch.randn(
   313→        (total_q, num_heads, QK_HEAD_DIM),
   314→        dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda", generator=gen,
   315→    ) * 0.02
   316→
   317→    # Compressed KV buffer: (total_kv, 1, 576) bf16 — the source of truth
   318→    kv_buffer_bf16 = torch.randn(
   319→        (total_kv, NUM_KV_HEADS, QK_HEAD_DIM),
   320→        dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda", generator=gen,
   321→    ) * 0.02
   322→
   323→    # Quantize KV to fp8
   324→    kv_buffer_fp8, kv_scale_fp8 = quantize_fp8(kv_buffer_bf16)
   325→
   326→    # Quantize KV to mxfp4
   327→    kv_buffer_mxfp4, kv_scale_mxfp4 = quantize_mxfp4(kv_buffer_bf16)
   328→
   329→    # All three KV formats: bf16 is a Tensor, fp8/mxfp4 are (Tensor, Tensor) tuples
   330→    kv_data = {
   331→        "bf16": kv_buffer_bf16,
   332→        "fp8": (kv_buffer_fp8, kv_scale_fp8),
   333→        "mxfp4": (kv_buffer_mxfp4, kv_scale_mxfp4),
   334→    }
   335→
   336→    qo_indptr = torch.arange(0, batchsize + 1, dtype=torch.int32, device="cuda") * qseqlen
   337→    kv_indptr = torch.arange(0, batchsize + 1, dtype=torch.int32, device="cuda") * kvseqlen
   338→
   339→    config = {
   340→        "batch_size": batchsize,
   341→        "num_heads": num_heads,
   342→        "num_kv_heads": NUM_KV_HEADS,
   343→        "qk_head_dim": QK_HEAD_DIM,
   344→        "kv_lora_rank": KV_LORA_RANK,
   345→        "qk_rope_head_dim": QK_ROPE_HEAD_DIM,
   346→        "v_head_dim": V_HEAD_DIM,
   347→        "q_seq_len": qseqlen,
   348→        "kv_seq_len": kvseqlen,
   349→        "sm_scale": SM_SCALE,
   350→    }
   351→
   352→    return (q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config)
   353→
   354→
   355→def ref_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
   356→    """Reference MLA decode attention. Uses Q_DTYPE and KV_DTYPE to select kernel variant."""
   357→    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config = data
   358→
   359→    # Resolve Q
   360→    if Q_DTYPE == "fp8":
   361→        q_input, q_scale = quantize_fp8(q)
   362→    else:
   363→        q_input, q_scale = q, None
   364→
   365→    # Resolve KV
   366→    if KV_DTYPE == "fp8":
   367→        kv_buffer_fp8, kv_scale = kv_data["fp8"]
   368→        kv_input = kv_buffer_fp8
   369→    else:
   370→        kv_input, kv_scale = kv_data["bf16"], None
   371→
   372→    return _aiter_mla_decode(
   373→        q_input, kv_input, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config,
   374→        q_scale=q_scale, kv_scale=kv_scale,
   375→    )
   376→
   377→
   378→check_implementation = make_match_reference(ref_kernel, rtol=1e-02, atol=1e-02)
   379→
```

     1→import torch
     2→from typing import TypeVar, TypedDict, Union
     3→
     4→# DeepSeek R1 MLA forward_absorb format:
     5→#
     6→# Input: (q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config)
     7→#   q:          (total_q, num_heads, qk_head_dim)   bfloat16
     8→#               num_heads = 128 // tp (tp=4 → 32, tp=8 → 16)
     9→#   kv_data:    dict with three KV cache formats:
    10→#     "bf16":   Tensor  (total_kv, 1, 576)           bfloat16
    11→#     "fp8":    (Tensor, Tensor)  kv_buffer fp8 (total_kv, 1, 576) + scalar scale
    12→#     "mxfp4":  (Tensor, Tensor)  kv_buffer fp4x2 (total_kv, 1, 288) + fp8_e8m0 scale
    13→#   qo_indptr:  (batch_size + 1,)                    int32
    14→#   kv_indptr:  (batch_size + 1,)                    int32
    15→#   config:     dict with MLA parameters (includes num_heads computed from tp)
    16→#
    17→# where qk_head_dim = kv_lora_rank + qk_rope_head_dim = 512 + 64 = 576
    18→#
    19→# Output: attention output tensor (total_q, num_heads, v_head_dim) bfloat16
    20→#   where v_head_dim = kv_lora_rank = 512
    21→#
    22→# The kv_buffer stores the compressed KV representation:
    23→#   - Full 576 dims used as keys (for Q@K^T score computation)
    24→#   - First 512 dims (kv_lora_rank) used as values (for output computation)
    25→
    26→input_t = TypeVar(
    27→    "input_t",
    28→    bound=tuple[torch.Tensor, dict, torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor, dict],
    29→)
    30→output_t = TypeVar("output_t", bound=torch.Tensor)
    31→
    32→
    33→class TestSpec(TypedDict):
    34→    batchsize: int
    35→    qseqlen: int
    36→    kvseqlen: int
    37→    tp: int

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Key discovery from reading the reference files:** The MoE `reference.py` uses `aiter.get_torch_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)` for weight quantization (line 109). The GEMM `reference.py` uses `aiter.get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)` with `shuffle=True` (line 95-96). The GEMM submission currently delegates to `ref_kernel` because of a Triton JIT call-site bug. But if we use `get_torch_quant` instead of `get_triton_quant` — the same function MoE's reference already uses — we might bypass the JIT bug entirely. This is the most actionable untested optimization.

Additionally, the MLA reference provides **three KV cache formats** (bf16, fp8, mxfp4) but we've only tried fp8. The mxfp4 format could be faster if aiter's MLA kernel has an MXFP4 path.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`
